# Market scan appendix

Generated from an 11-agent research workflow. 134 apps catalogued, 73 gaps identified across 5 analytical lenses.

## Apps catalogued

| App | Origin | Category | AI use (real vs marketing) | Pricing |

Snap is real image recognition trained on ~150,000 Indian food items, but | India tiers (per secondary sources, FLAG - not vendor-confirmed): Smart Plan ~INR 208/mo; single-coach plan INR 1,500+/mo; Pro with CGM INR 5,500-8,000/mo. | Calorie Mama (Azumio) | USA (Azumio Inc.) | Early photo-recognition app + developer Food AI API | Classical CNN food-image classification → database lookup. Pre-LLM architecture: it names the dish, then looks up a canonical portion. | Consumer app freemium; API is developer-licensed, tiered. |
| Bite AI | USA (NYC, founded 2016) | Food recognition / food intelligence API (B2B) | CNN-based food detection and recognition API. Pre-LLM generation, similar architecture class to Calorie Mama. | API, commercial. Not verified. |
| Nutrino | Israel (Tel Aviv) | Personalised nutrition analytics — ACQUIRED, no longer available | ML for personalised glycemic/nutritional response prediction, not primarily food-image recognition. | N/A — no longer a standalone commercial product. |
| Samsung Food (formerly Whisk) | South Korea / UK (Whisk was UK-based, acquired by Samsung 2019, relaunched as Samsung Food 2023) | Recipe-and-meal-planning platform with vision bolt-on; ecosystem play | Vision AI calorie estimation — but reportedly gated to Galaxy devices, which makes it a hardware-ecosystem feature rather than a portable capability. | Free with Samsung account; premium tier exists. |
| Open Food Facts | France (non-profit, open data) | Free open barcode + nutrition database — your realistic packaged-food backend | None — it is a database, not an AI. That is a feature: it is ground truth you can anchor a model against. | Free. Open data (ODbL). |
| MyFCD (Malaysian Food Composition Database, MOH) | Malaysia (Ministry of Health, Nutrition Division) | Official national food composition database — the ONLY authoritative Malaysian source | None. Static reference database with a web UI. | Free to browse at myfcd.moh.gov.my. |

| Oura | Finland (Oulu) | Recovery/readiness ring | Genuine ML in sleep staging and in the 2025-26 heart-health features: Cardiovascular Age derived from pulse wave velocity, and Cardio Capacity (VO2max estimate). | Garmin Connect (Garmin Coach / Training Readiness) | USA/Switzerland; algorithms from Firstbeat Analytics (Jyväskylä, Finland, acquired 2020) | Adaptive endurance plans + physiological modelling | Mostly deterministic physiological modelling (EPOC-based Training Effect, HRV-status baselines, VO2max estimation from HR/pace decoupling) rather than learned models. Firstbeat's methods are at least  | Free software; requires Garmin hardware ($200-1,200) |
| Apple Fitness+ / watchOS Training Load | USA (Cupertino) | Content subscription + first-party load tracking | The effort ESTIMATOR is a real regression model. Workout Buddy is a genuine on-device LLM + custom TTS. Custom Plans is closer to a preferences-to-template mapper. Overall: modest, honest, well-scoped | Fitness+ ~$9.99/mo or $79.99/yr; Training Load is free with the watch |
| Samsung Health AI Coach | South Korea (Suwon) | Watch-native coaching | Energy Score and the running-level classifier are ML-flavoured composites; the coaching layer is increasingly LLM-generated advice text. The 12-minute assessment is a real, validated-in-principle fiel | Free with Galaxy Watch hardware |
| Athlytic | USA (independent developer) — UNVERIFIED | Whoop-style recovery from Apple Watch data | None meaningful. It is baseline-deviation arithmetic over HealthKit data, presented well. That is not a criticism — it is the honest version of what Whoop sells with hardware. | ~$4.99/mo or ~$29.99/yr (verify current) |
| Gentler Streak | Slovenia (Ljubljana) — Gentler Stories | Well-being-first activity guidance | None. It is a rolling-window load model with HRV modulation and a carefully written copy layer. | Fitbod | USA (San Francisco) | Algorithmic strength programming | Partly real, mostly overclaimed. | JuggernautAI | USA (Juggernaut Training Systems — Chad Wesley Smith; engine by Tim Arnold) | Autoregulated powerlifting/powerbuilding programming | 'AI' is a stretch; it is expert-system autoregulation — a well-tuned rule set encoding Scientific Principles of Strength Training, driven by user feedback. That is not a knock: it is more defensible t | ~$35/mo (verify current) |

| Strong | Unclear — UNVERIFIED | Workout logger | None. Zero. It is a very good database front-end and has never pretended otherwise. | $29.99/yr or $99.99 lifetime |
| Boostcamp | USA — UNVERIFIED | Program library + auto-progression logger | None claimed. Deterministic percentage-of-training-max progression. It is the control group that shows how much of 'AI programming' is arithmetic. | Generous free tier; paid tier modest |
| Runna | UK (London); acquired by Strava (USA), announced April 2025 | Adaptive running plans | Adaptation is rules over recent performance and feedback, not learned models. | Zing Coach | UK — UNVERIFIED | CV-first AI personal trainer | Real, on two axes: a CNN for body composition from photos, and pose estimation for reps/form. Among software-only apps this has the highest ratio of actual computer vision to marketing. | ~$14.99/mo or ~$89.99/yr (verify) |
| SensAI (sensai.fit) | Unclear — UNVERIFIED | Wearable-reading adaptive training | Claimed recovery-driven plan rewriting. Mechanism undocumented. Treat as unproven. | ~$96/yr per its own comparison content — self-reported |
| Ray (rayfit.com) | Unclear — UNVERIFIED | Real-time voice AI trainer | Genuine LLM + speech + rep detection. This is a real product category emerging in 2026 and it is where combat-gym coaching would naturally live — you cannot look at a screen while working pads. | Unverified |
| HRV4Training | Netherlands/Italy (Marco Altini) | Phone-camera HRV and readiness — NOT in the original brief, but the single most relevant product to a phone-only sensor constraint | Signal processing (peak detection on the PPG waveform), not ML. No camera, no pose estimation. | Hardware bundles ~$439-1,349 + ~$39/mo membership |
| Welling / MyDietPRO | Welling: Asia-focused; MyDietPRO: Malaysia | Localized SEA/Malaysian nutrition tracking — the direct local competition for /fuel | Vision models fine-tuned or prompted for Asian cuisine, plus curated local food databases. The database is the actual asset; the model is commodity. | Varies; generally below Western competitors |

## Gaps, ranked by boldness

### [5/5 · medium] Churn prediction priced on outcome — the one metric a gym owner is actually paid on, and the one thing every platform either lacks or has permanently 'coming soon'

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Hapana's pricing page lists churn prediction inside 'Hapana AI', marked *coming soon* and free for three months on activation — i.e. not shipped and not yet monetisable. PushPress's equivalent is 'Member Intel', which surfaces 'Milestones, new faces, and moments worth acting on' plus attendance trending — pattern-spotting on check-ins only. Vibefam's page mentions no retention analytics at all. All of them are priced per location or per tier; none is priced on churn actually prevented, because none of them can measure the whole loop at one site.

**Who fails it:** Hapana (unshipped), PushPress (attendance-only signal), Glofox/Mindbody (reporting, not prediction), Vibefam (none). Also every consumer app — they optimise DAU, which is not what a gym sells.

**Hybrid play:** Build a Revenue-at-Risk board denominated in MYR, not in engagement. Score decay across four signals only HUD has: attendance gaps vs the member's own baseline, intensity decay (XP/round volume, not steps), form-score decline, and /fuel logging drop-off. Output a named list with a MYR figure ('RM1,440/month at risk across 8 members'). Then price the product against it: RM250/mo base plus RM40 per member flagged-at-risk who returns within 30 days. Prove it with a holdout cohort — only possible because you control one whole gym. Three saved memberships at ~RM180/mo pays for the year.

---

### [5/5 · large] Gym-vs-gym rivalry is the only growth loop a horizontal app can never build — and in combat sports it is already the culture, so it doubles as the B2B distribution channel

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Klang Valley has a healthy, competitive combat scene — TRIBE, Monarchy MMA, Panthera Muay Thai, Team Thoo, Japan Boxing Club KLCC — and interclub/smoker culture means inter-gym rivalry is native, not manufactured. Every existing incentive layer in Malaysia (Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, AIA Vitality, WeWard) is step-count based, which means 'a 90-minute Muay Thai session, a heavy squat day, or 200 rounds on the bag produce almost no steps and therefore score near zero everywhere' — combat athletes are systematically invisible to every national incentive scheme. Nothing exists that scores combat work at all, let alone competitively between gyms.

**Who fails it:** ClassPass actively works against gym identity by training members to shop by credit price. Wellhub is not meaningfully present in SEA at all. PERKESO Activ@Work and AIA Vitality can only count steps. No platform ranks gyms against each other on verified training output.

**Hybrid play:** Ship the Klang Valley Combat Ladder: a monthly cross-gym table of verified training volume — rounds logged, XP, form-score averages — normalised per active member so a 40-member gym can beat a 400-member chain. Hybrid Combative runs it and is on it. Rival gyms must install HUD to appear, which converts rivalry into free B2B distribution and makes the second, third and tenth gym inbound rather than sold. Ladder seasons end with a physical interclub at one of the gyms. This is the single highest-leverage thing in the whole document: it is a network effect that only a locally-embedded operator can start.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Extreme locality beats database scale on food accuracy — this membership eats at roughly thirty places, and thirty calibrated dishes will out-perform a ten-thousand-dish national database

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** MalaysianCalorie ships 10,000+ local dishes and still cannot solve portion estimation for nasi campur, shared plates and gravy-heavy mamak food — no accuracy claims are published by anyone. FatSecret's crowd-sourced Malaysian data returns nasi lemak at 145 kcal/100g, 261 kcal/serving and 348 kcal/cup simultaneously, which the research correctly says 'destroys trust for anyone actually cutting'. The failure is variance between stalls, not absence of entries — and stall variance is exactly what disappears when you fix the geography to a 2km radius around one gym.

**Who fails it:** MyFitnessPal (measured −327 kcal), Cal AI (−345 kcal), FatSecret (inconsistent), MalaysianCalorie and Berry Best (breadth without calibration), MyNutriDiari (one generic 'nasi lemak' entry for the whole country).

**Hybrid play:** Build the Damansara Canteen: the 30–50 dishes this membership actually eats, from the specific stalls they actually use, each weighed and calibrated once by the coach with a RM40 kitchen scale, stored with photos at real portion sizes from the real plates. Vision then classifies against a tiny closed set instead of estimating open-world, which is a dramatically easier problem and can run with far higher confidence. Members pick 'Ali Maju, nasi campur, 2 lauk' not 'mixed rice, generic'. It also cuts data cost on prepaid plans, which the research flags as real friction. Every new gym gets its own canteen — which is a per-location asset that compounds and cannot be copied by anyone not standing in that carpark.

---

### [5/5 · small] Malaysian money plumbing is a moat, not a chore — recurring TnG billing went live in March 2026 and the RM500 LHDN sports relief is an annually-renewing retention hook that literally no fitness app uses

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Research confirms ~85–90% Malaysian adult e-wallet adoption, Touch 'n Go eWallet at 23M+ verified users with recurring billing supported as of March 2026, and FPX/DuitNow QR as the workhorse rails. It also confirms USD-billed subscriptions are 'actively resented' (the standing complaint against MyFitnessPal and Strava) and that Vibefam's whole competitive pitch against Mindbody and Glofox is local payment-rail support. Separately: 'the LHDN sports lifestyle tax relief of up to RM500/year covering qualifying gym memberships and equipment — a real, concrete, annually-renewing hook that no app I found exploits well'.

**Who fails it:** Every foreign platform on card-only billing — PushPress (CC/ACH), Glofox, Mindbody, MyFitnessPal, Strava, ClassPass. And every Malaysian fitness app, none of which touches LHDN.

**Hybrid play:** Bill the *gym* in MYR via FPX/Curlec or Billplz; let members top up any paid extras via TnG or DuitNow QR; never show a USD number. Then build the Tax Dossier: every January, auto-generate each member's LHDN-ready receipt bundle for the prior year — membership fees, class packs, qualifying equipment — as a single PDF the gym emails out. It costs almost nothing to build, it is genuinely useful, it fires in exactly the window where gym churn peaks and New Year renewals are decided, and it gives the owner a legitimate reason to contact every lapsed member once a year. It also quietly raises the effective price of the membership by up to RM500 in the member's mind.

---

### [5/5 · medium] The Fatigue-vs-Failure joint-pain data is an owner-facing dataset that does not exist anywhere in the industry — programming feedback, insurance evidence and a genuine duty-of-care artefact

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** /home-base already collects tap-where-it-hurts joint pain and queues corrective drills. Nothing in the surveyed market collects anything comparable at population level: gym platforms record attendance and payments, consumer apps record calories and steps, and the only serious rehab reference point in Malaysia is home physiotherapy at ~RM250/session (Homage) — which the research correctly warns means an app-based corrective drill must never position itself as a substitute. Aggregated across a membership, this is a signal an owner has literally never had access to.

**Who fails it:** All of them. PushPress, Hapana, Glofox, Mindbody, Vibefam, Zen Planner — none model injury or discomfort. Consumer apps (Berry Best, MalaysianCalorie, HealthifyMe) have no movement layer at all.

**Hybrid play:** Build an anonymised Floor Heatmap for the owner: joint-complaint incidence by body region, plotted against programming blocks and coaches over time — 'shoulder complaints up 3x in the four weeks after the new conditioning block was added; concentrated in Coach A's Thursday class.' Two commercial consequences: it is a programming feedback loop that makes the head coach demonstrably better, and it is documented duty-of-care evidence for insurance and for defending against an injury claim. Ship it with hard guardrails — never diagnose, always route persistent joint pain to a named physio the gym has a referral relationship with (which is also a small revenue share). Keep the raw pain data on-device or PDPA-scoped and say so loudly; the research flags that Malaysians are increasingly sensitive to health data leaving the country, so on-device processing is a marketing asset.

---

### [5/5 · medium] The microphone is a 1,400x-faster sensor than the camera, and no fitness product uses it. Combat sport is an impact sport, and impacts are acoustic transients that 30fps vision physically cannot resolve.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. Six of the Malaysian nutrition apps and four of the regional ones do food-photo-to-macros — and the NIH/ASN 2026 study measured all four leading photo apps underestimating meal energy by roughly 33% (Cal AI worst at -345 kcal). HealthifyMe's own stated Snap accuracy is 60-75% on its home cuisine. Meanwhile the capabilities that are genuinely superhuman are entirely unclaimed in Malaysia and thinly claimed globally.

A human coach's binding constraints are: (a) they are present for ~3 hours of your week and zero of your meals; (b) their sensing resolution is one pair of eyes at ~10Hz, watching one athlete at a time; (c) their memory is recency-weighted anecdote, not n=400 sessions; (d) they cannot run the future before you live it; (e) their income depends on you, which caps their honesty. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.

Four structural advantages, in order of defensibility for a solo dev with a phone:

1. RESOLUTION. The phone already contains a sensor 1,400x faster than its camera — the microphone — and no fitness product in this corpus uses it. Combat sport is an impact sport; impacts are acoustic transients. 30fps vision literally skips the contact frame of a jab.
2. PRESENCE. Being at every rep AND every meal for the same person for years makes N-of-1 causal inference possible, which is the one thing no dietitian or coach can do. Critically, this does NOT require accurate calories — a systematic bias cancels in a within-subject model. The category is losing an accuracy war it did not need to fight.
3. RECALL + CROSS-REFERENCE. Longitudinal per-joint baselining turns pain from a lagging indicator into a leading one, and turns a gym of 100 bodies into a longitudinal cohort study with known outcomes.
4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. A camera+mic that objectively counts 412 strikes over 47 minutes IS auto-captured sensor data. Hybrid can be the wearable. That is the only route to being net cash-positive for the user, which the RM10/mo AIA anchor and KFit's RM99 failure make mandatory.

Architecture consequence: everything continuous and identity-bearing — pose, audio, PPG — stays on-device, both because mid-range Android on prepaid data cannot afford otherwise and because PDPA-era Malaysians will not send body video offshore. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** Camera pose runs at ~30fps = 33ms temporal resolution; phone audio runs at 44.1kHz = 0.023ms, roughly 1,400x finer. A jab's contact event lasts a few milliseconds and is frequently skipped entirely between video frames. Percussive onset detection (spectral flux) is a solved, cheap DSP problem that runs in WebAudio on-device with a fraction of the battery and thermal cost of continuous vision — which directly addresses the corpus's mid-range-Android thermal-throttling and prepaid-data constraints. The corpus also notes gym lighting is dim/flickering and basements have no signal: audio degrades gracefully in exactly the conditions that break vision and OCR.

**Who fails it:** Every app in both research passes. Hybrid's own Shadow Dojo is MediaPipe-only and therefore 30fps-blind to contact. FightCamp/Corner-style products solve this only by selling paid hand sensors, which the no-wearable constraint rules out. No Malaysian app does movement CV at all.

**Hybrid play:** 'Bag Mode': phone face-down on the bench or in a pocket, mic only, no camera. Onset detection yields strike count, inter-strike interval, per-round cadence curve, and a normalised impact-energy proxy. Ship three metrics no Malaysian product has ever shown a member: strikes per round, cadence decay across rounds (the real conditioning number), and left/right rhythm asymmetry from inter-onset interval clustering. When the camera IS propped, fuse: audio timestamps the contact to the millisecond, vision supplies the kinematics of the three frames before it. This single feature makes a Muay Thai round measurable without any hardware, in the dark, with a dead-flat battery budget.

---

### [5/5 · medium] The entire nutrition-AI category is fighting an accuracy war it did not need to fight. For within-subject causal inference, a systematic bias cancels — you do not need correct calories, you need consistent ones.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. Six of the Malaysian nutrition apps and four of the regional ones do food-photo-to-macros — and the NIH/ASN 2026 study measured all four leading photo apps underestimating meal energy by roughly 33% (Cal AI worst at -345 kcal). HealthifyMe's own stated Snap accuracy is 60-75% on its home cuisine. Meanwhile the capabilities that are genuinely superhuman are entirely unclaimed in Malaysia and thinly claimed globally.

A human coach's binding constraints are: (a) they are present for ~3 hours of your week and zero of your meals; (b) their sensing resolution is one pair of eyes at ~10Hz, watching one athlete at a time; (c) their memory is recency-weighted anecdote, not n=400 sessions; (d) they cannot run the future before you live it; (e) their income depends on you, which caps their honesty. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.

Four structural advantages, in order of defensibility for a solo dev with a phone:

1. RESOLUTION. The phone already contains a sensor 1,400x faster than its camera — the microphone — and no fitness product in this corpus uses it. Combat sport is an impact sport; impacts are acoustic transients. 30fps vision literally skips the contact frame of a jab.
2. PRESENCE. Being at every rep AND every meal for the same person for years makes N-of-1 causal inference possible, which is the one thing no dietitian or coach can do. Critically, this does NOT require accurate calories — a systematic bias cancels in a within-subject model. The category is losing an accuracy war it did not need to fight.
3. RECALL + CROSS-REFERENCE. Longitudinal per-joint baselining turns pain from a lagging indicator into a leading one, and turns a gym of 100 bodies into a longitudinal cohort study with known outcomes.
4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. A camera+mic that objectively counts 412 strikes over 47 minutes IS auto-captured sensor data. Hybrid can be the wearable. That is the only route to being net cash-positive for the user, which the RM10/mo AIA anchor and KFit's RM99 failure make mandatory.

Architecture consequence: everything continuous and identity-bearing — pose, audio, PPG — stays on-device, both because mid-range Android on prepaid data cannot afford otherwise and because PDPA-era Malaysians will not send body video offshore. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** NIH/ASN 2026 (Hengist et al., 102 nutrient-controlled meals, NUTRITION 2026): all four leading photo apps underestimated energy by ~33% — Cal AI -345 kcal, Lose It! -333, MyFitnessPal -327, Appediet -252, plus ~30g fat under across the board. HealthifyMe's own reported Snap accuracy is 60-70%, rising to ~75% with human-in-the-loop, on Indian food it has 150,000 training items for. But if the model under-reads nasi lemak by 30% every single time, it remains a perfectly serviceable predictor variable in a per-user fixed-effects regression — the offset drops out. Nobody in either research pass has noticed this.

**Who fails it:** MalaysianCalorie, Berry Best, MyKalori, MyDietPRO, BookDoc food log, HealthifyMe, Cal AI, MyFitnessPal, FatSecret — all stop at the log and all compete on an accuracy claim that independent testing has now discredited. Symmetrically, none of the training-side products (Strong, Hevy, Fitness First MyCoach, Berry Best Gym Mode) has any food data. Nobody in this market holds both halves on one user ID.

**Hybrid play:** 'Fuel → Output attribution'. Once a member has ~20 paired meal/session records, fit a regularised per-user model of pre-session meal features (macro split, timing offset in hours, dish class, kopitiam vs home) against session performance features (rep velocity decay, strike cadence decay from Bag Mode, console watts from /scanner). Ship one card a month: 'the strongest single predictor of your bad sessions this month was X. n=27, controlling for round count.' Then market the honest claim as a weapon: Hybrid does not claim 97% calorie accuracy like Cal AI — it claims relative consistency, which is the only property the inference actually requires. This is a defensible, testable, publicly-verifiable position in a category whose accuracy marketing just collapsed.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Pain is a lagging indicator; kinematic asymmetry is a leading one. Nobody runs per-joint statistical process control on an individual athlete's own longitudinal baseline, so every corrective feature in existence is reactive.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.

Critically, this does NOT require accurate calories — a systematic bias cancels in a within-subject model. The category is losing an accuracy war it did not need to fight.
3. RECALL + CROSS-REFERENCE. Longitudinal per-joint baselining turns pain from a lagging indicator into a leading one, and turns a gym of 100 bodies into a longitudinal cohort study with known outcomes.
4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. That is the only route to being net cash-positive for the user, which the RM10/mo AIA anchor and KFit's RM99 failure make mandatory.

Architecture consequence: everything continuous and identity-bearing — pose, audio, PPG — stays on-device, both because mid-range Android on prepaid data cannot afford otherwise and because PDPA-era Malaysians will not send body video offshore. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** A coach sees an athlete ~3h/week and holds no numeric baseline of hip drop, knee valgus under fatigue, guard height or teep chamber angle. An on-device pose model recording every rep for 18 months holds all of them, per side, per fatigue state. Asymmetry and dynamic valgus under fatigue are established injury-risk correlates in the ACL/screening literature; the novelty here is not the biomarker but running it as a per-athlete change-point detector (CUSUM/EWMA) over months rather than as a one-off clinic screening. Hybrid already collects the downstream half of this — the 'tap where it hurts' Fatigue vs Failure check — but only uses it after the athlete already hurts.

**Who fails it:** Everyone. No app in either pass does any movement CV in Malaysia. Globally, form-check products score the current rep and forget it. Fitness First's Boditrax bioimpedance data is trapped in-club and is body composition, not movement. Vibefam records belt rank, which is a coach's declaration, not a measurement.

**Hybrid play:** 'Drift Watch'. Store per-session distributions (not video — a few KB of landmark statistics) of ~8 kinematic invariants per side. Run EWMA change-point detection per joint. When a limb crosses threshold, never diagnose: surface the trend chart, pre-emptively queue the corrective drill before pain is reported, and set a hard escalation rule — sustained drift plus a reported pain event triggers the clinic export (see the physio handover gap). This is the single highest-value use of pose data Hybrid is already capturing and discarding.

### [5/5 · small] Every AI form-checker on earth cues on every rep forever. Motor-learning research says that is precisely the design that degrades long-term retention. Nobody implements a deliberate feedback-withdrawal schedule — the AI shutting up on purpose.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. A human coach's binding constraints are: (a) they are present for ~3 hours of your week and zero of your meals; (b) their sensing resolution is one pair of eyes at ~10Hz, watching one athlete at a time; (c) their memory is recency-weighted anecdote, not n=400 sessions; (d) they cannot run the future before you live it; (e) their income depends on you, which caps their honesty. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.

Critically, this does NOT require accurate calories — a systematic bias cancels in a within-subject model. The category is losing an accuracy war it did not need to fight.
3. RECALL + CROSS-REFERENCE. Longitudinal per-joint baselining turns pain from a lagging indicator into a leading one, and turns a gym of 100 bodies into a longitudinal cohort study with known outcomes.
4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. A camera+mic that objectively counts 412 strikes over 47 minutes IS auto-captured sensor data. Hybrid can be the wearable. That is the only route to being net cash-positive for the user, which the RM10/mo AIA anchor and KFit's RM99 failure make mandatory.

Architecture consequence: everything continuous and identity-bearing — pose, audio, PPG — stays on-device, both because mid-range Android on prepaid data cannot afford otherwise and because PDPA-era Malaysians will not send body video offshore. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** The guidance hypothesis (Salmoni/Schmidt/Walter; Winstein & Schmidt reduced-frequency KR studies) holds that concurrent augmented feedback improves performance during practice while impairing retention, because the learner becomes dependent on the external cue. Reduced-frequency, bandwidth and summary feedback schedules produce worse practice curves and better retention. A good human coach fades cues by intuition but cannot do it systematically, cannot measure whether the skill consolidated, and has a commercial incentive to keep being needed. An AI can run a formal schedule and empirically test consolidation with silent probe sets. (Flag: this is established motor-learning literature asserted from knowledge — I could not re-verify citations in this pass; worth a 30-minute check before it becomes marketing copy.)

**Who fails it:** The entire global form-check category, not just Malaysia. Every product treats more feedback as more product value — it is the thing they demo. Apple Fitness+ is broadcast-only and cannot see you at all. Train With Jordan is pre-recorded video with no feedback loop.

**Hybrid play:** Shadow Dojo 'Coaching Ladder'. Each movement pattern carries a per-user feedback state: ACQUIRE (cue every rep) → BANDWIDTH (cue only outside a widening error tolerance) → SUMMARY (silent during the set, report after) → SILENT PROBE (no cue at all; score retention). This slots perfectly into the existing RPG framing — a technique is not levelled until you can perform it clean with the coach silent — and it creates a progression axis that is genuinely novel rather than cosmetic. Cheapest item on this list to build (scheduling logic over existing pose scoring) and the hardest to copy, because copying it requires believing something counterintuitive about your own product.

---

### [5/5 · large] A gym is a closed population of bodies that repeatedly collide with each other, generating pairwise observations nobody records. No coach on earth tracks a matchup matrix across 40 members over 3 years.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. **Evidence:** Every product in the corpus that touches a combat gym — Vibefam, Mindbody, Glofox, ClassPass, TRIBE's app — records bookings, attendance and at best belt rank. The corpus's own summary line is decisive: 'Not one of them tracks what the athlete actually did.' None of them records who fought whom, and therefore none can condition your performance on opponent archetype. Head coaches pair sparring partners by memory and by who happens to be standing nearby.

**Who fails it:** Vibefam (belt rank is a coach's declaration, not a measurement), Mindbody/Glofox, ClassPass (bookings only, and it actively fragments members across studios so no continuity exists), every chain gym app.

**Hybrid play:** Log every sparring round as a consented pair. Even from one camera you can derive relative height, stance (orthodox/southpaw) and — via Bag Mode audio — per-athlete strike cadence. Two outputs: (1) a personal Matchup Profile — your output, cadence and guard integrity conditioned on opponent archetype ('your cadence drops 22% against southpaws and only against southpaws'); (2) a coach-facing Pairing Recommender that assigns tonight's partners to maximise each member's learning signal subject to hard safety constraints on weight, experience and injury flags. That second one is a real constrained-optimisation problem, it is solved badly by every gym in the world today, and it makes the head coach visibly better at the most consequential decision they make each session.

### [5/5 · large] Pose data is a reusable asset and everyone throws it away after scoring the rep. A recorded skeleton can be replayed as a training partner — including your own past self — available at 11pm on a Tuesday, forever, at the exact tempo of the real person.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

**Evidence:** MediaPipe landmarks are ~33 points at 30fps — a few KB per second, storable and replayable on a mid-range Android where video would not be. That storage economics is what makes this feasible for a solo dev on a Supabase free-ish tier, and it is precisely why nobody with a video-first architecture has done it. A coach can demonstrate a technique once, in person, at their convenience; a ghost is on demand and does not get tired or leave the gym.

**Who fails it:** No product in this market holds member pose data at all. Apple Fitness+ is broadcast video with no sensing (and requires an Apple Watch, ruling it out on Hybrid's constraint anyway). Train With Jordan is pre-recorded content. Keep may have camera features domestically in China but withdrew from global markets after 2019.

**Hybrid play:** /home-base 'Ghost' mode — overlay a stored skeleton on the live camera and score timing alignment. Three variants, in ascending strategic value: PAST SELF (your PB round replayed as a pacing target — the strongest possible progression narrative for the RPG layer, and it needs zero content production); REFERENCE GHOST (the head coach's technique captured once and reusable forever, turning the coach's skill into a durable gym asset); OPPONENT GHOST (an upcoming interclub opponent's rhythm captured on a phone at their gym). The retention consequence is the real point: leaving the gym means losing your ghosts, your past selves and your coach's library. That is a switching cost no booking app can construct.
