**Evidence:** Corpus: Ramadan training shifts to roughly an hour pre-iftar or to 9:30pm–midnight post-terawih; volume falls to 3-4 sessions/week at 30-45 minutes; hydration compresses to iftar→sahur. Hari Raya means a week-plus of kampung travel; CNY, Deepavali, school holidays, monsoon and haze season each produce predictable collapses. Only two products in ~50 show any Ramadan awareness at all — MalaysianCalorie (nutrition) and JomRun (one virtual run) — and NEITHER touches training. The corpus's own verdict: 'a streak system that penalises a fasting Muslim for missing daytime sessions is culturally broken.' All programming in this market is either pre-authored content or a human writing a block from experience and discovering in week 6 that it was wrong.

**Who fails it:** Train With Jordan and Apple Fitness+ (pre-authored, no adaptation). Fittr, Naluri, Joompa (human coaches, no simulation). Every gym app (booking only). Nobody, anywhere in this corpus, models adherence probability.

**Hybrid play:** 'Block Sim': before committing an 8-week block, run 1,000 forward simulations using the member's own adherence hazard model (day-of-week, days-since-last-session, weather, calendar collisions) and their own observed fatigue response, then show three curves — expected, p10, p90 — and a single headline: 'this plan has a 34% chance of surviving contact with Ramadan.' Let the athlete choose by robustness rather than ambition. Auto-insert known collisions as first-class plan objects: pre-Ramadan taper, in-Ramadan 3x30min post-terawih template, Raya kampung bodyweight block, post-Raya re-entry ramp — with rest shields auto-applying during fasting instead of punishing it. No competitor can respond to this without rebuilding their entire programming layer.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Real gyms contain a permanent unresolved argument — head coach versus S&C coach versus physio versus nutritionist — and the athlete never gets to hear it. They get one person's opinion, filtered by whoever they asked. A multi-agent system can stage the argument and show the disagreement instead of smoothing it into a single confident answer.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. Six of the Malaysian nutrition apps and four of the regional ones do food-photo-to-macros — and the NIH/ASN 2026 study measured all four leading photo apps underestimating meal energy by roughly 33% (Cal AI worst at -345 kcal). HealthifyMe's own stated Snap accuracy is 60-75% on its home cuisine. Meanwhile the capabilities that are genuinely superhuman are entirely unclaimed in Malaysia and thinly claimed globally.

A human coach's binding constraints are: (a) they are present for ~3 hours of your week and zero of your meals; (b) their sensing resolution is one pair of eyes at ~10Hz, watching one athlete at a time; (c) their memory is recency-weighted anecdote, not n=400 sessions; (d) they cannot run the future before you live it; (e) their income depends on you, which caps their honesty. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.

Four structural advantages, in order of defensibility for a solo dev with a phone:

1. RESOLUTION. The phone already contains a sensor 1,400x faster than its camera — the microphone — and no fitness product in this corpus uses it. Combat sport is an impact sport; impacts are acoustic transients. 30fps vision literally skips the contact frame of a jab.
2. PRESENCE. Being at every rep AND every meal for the same person for years makes N-of-1 causal inference possible, which is the one thing no dietitian or coach can do. Critically, this does NOT require accurate calories — a systematic bias cancels in a within-subject model. The category is losing an accuracy war it did not need to fight.
3. RECALL + CROSS-REFERENCE. Longitudinal per-joint baselining turns pain from a lagging indicator into a leading one, and turns a gym of 100 bodies into a longitudinal cohort study with known outcomes.
4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. A camera+mic that objectively counts 412 strikes over 47 minutes IS auto-captured sensor data. Hybrid can be the wearable. That is the only route to being net cash-positive for the user, which the RM10/mo AIA anchor and KFit's RM99 failure make mandatory.

Architecture consequence: everything continuous and identity-bearing — pose, audio, PPG — stays on-device, both because mid-range Android on prepaid data cannot afford otherwise and because PDPA-era Malaysians will not send body video offshore. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** Every AI coaching product in both passes converges on one authoritative voice, which is exactly where LLM overconfidence does most damage — and this market has already been burned by rented, overclaimed AI (Prudential Pulse licensed Babylon, Tictrac and AIME, and Babylon has since collapsed; Joompa and Vibefam both market 'AI' with no described mechanism). Structured disagreement is also a genuine accuracy technique: independent specialist passes over the same evidence surface conflicts that a single pass papers over. Nothing in either research pass does anything like this.

**Who fails it:** Naluri (human coaches, one voice per domain, no cross-talk exposed to the member). Fittr (coach-dependent quality by design). Berry Best's chat coach. Joompa's undescribed 'AI programme generation.' Every product presents a single confident recommendation.

**Hybrid play:** Run five cheap specialist passes over the same monthly digest — Striking, Strength, Rehab, Fuel and a Skeptic whose only job is to attack the other four's evidence base — then surface a 'Panel' card that shows where they AGREE, where they CONFLICT, and what data would settle it. Example output: 'Striking wants your volume up 20% for the interclub. Rehab flags 6 weeks of left-knee drift. Skeptic notes the drift signal is based on 9 sessions and may be camera-angle artefact. Suggested resolution: two fixed-angle sessions this week to confirm.' This is honest in a way no human coaching relationship can be, it turns model uncertainty from a liability into the feature, and it directly earns the tactical-telemetry aesthetic — a HUD should show conflicting sensor readings, not a single reassuring number.

---

### [5/5 · medium] There is no plateau protocol anywhere in the market. The ~3-month churn cliff IS the plateau, and every app's response to flat numbers is to show the user flat numbers with more encouraging adjectives.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** The brief: ~70% quit within three months. That timing is not a coincidence — it is when beginner adaptation ends and progress goes non-linear, so an app whose entire feedback signal is 'number went up' abruptly has nothing true to say. In operant terms the reward schedule silently switches from continuous reinforcement to extinction, and extinction is the textbook producer of response abandonment. Kivetz/Urminsky/Zheng's post-reward resetting compounds it: each collected level makes the next one feel further away. Nothing in the entire five-researcher pack detects, names or responds to a plateau — not one product. Naluri, HealthifyMe and Fittr all solve it with a human coach noticing, which is expensive and is precisely what a single gym can do cheaply.

**Who fails it:** Every app in the pack. Berry Best (weight trend charts — a plateau renders as a flat line, i.e. the demoralising artefact itself). Fitness First MyCoach. Strava. HealthifyMe (solves it with paid human coaches — which is why it costs what it costs). ClassPass, TRIBE, Vibefam (no performance data at all, so a plateau is undetectable by construction).

**Hybrid play:** A STALL DETECTOR that fires deliberately rather than reassuringly. Three weeks of flat form scores or flat XP triggers a mandatory re-frame screen that CHANGES THE SCORING RULE rather than the encouragement copy: switch the athlete's primary axis, unlock a new technique track, or run a filmed RE-BASELINE — a 2-minute round scored head-to-head against their own 90-day-old round, which almost always reveals improvement the weekly numbers hid. Wrap it in the game grammar the product already speaks: in a well-designed game a stall means you need a NEW MECHANIC, not more grinding — so name the moment ('LEVEL GATE: PRESTIGE AVAILABLE') and make it feel earned. Then the commercial half: push a PLATEAU LIST to the coach every Monday. Detecting the 3-month cliff three weeks before it happens is the feature the gym owner will actually pay for, and it is the one no booking SaaS in this market can build.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Injury is the largest unmodelled cause of churn, and the app's own Fatigue-vs-Failure check currently ends at a corrective drill — it identifies the churn event and then hands the athlete back to a product that keeps punishing them for the layoff it just diagnosed.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** A joint-pain layoff produces exactly the 10-21 day gap that maps onto the brief's two-week / 75% quit cliff, and during that gap every existing app keeps the streak ticking down, keeps prescribing sessions the athlete cannot do, and keeps sending push notifications — which is about the most reliable uninstall trigger in consumer software. Malaysian economics make the alternative stark: the researchers price home physiotherapy at RM250/session (Homage) and note the correct guardrail — 'an app-based corrective drill must be careful never to position itself as a substitute' for real rehab. So the realistic counterfactual is not 'user goes to physio instead', it is 'user does nothing for six weeks and never comes back.' No product in the pack has an injured state at all.

**Who fails it:** All of them. AIA Vitality (points stop; no injured state, so a genuine injury reads identically to laziness). Activ@Work/KOSPEN (steps stop). ClassPass (credits burn during the layoff). TRIBE (class pack expires). Vibefam/Mindbody (attendance simply goes to zero and the gym finds out when the member doesn't renew). Berry Best, HealthifyMe, Fittr — no injury awareness.

**Hybrid play:** INJURED MODE as a first-class product state, not an edge case. When Fatigue-vs-Failure flags the same joint twice, the whole HUD reconfigures: the streak converts into a PROTECTED REHAB STREAK that pays XP for corrective drills; the affected radar axis greys out with a lock icon instead of visibly collapsing; and — the genuinely novel part — MediaPipe measures RANGE OF MOTION as the progress metric (shoulder abduction angle, knee flexion, hip internal rotation, scored from a 10-second camera check). That turns a layoff from a period of zero data into a period of visible daily progress, using a sensor the product already ships, and it is something no app in this market can copy without on-device pose. Return-to-train unlocks on measured ROM, not on the calendar. Hard guardrail, non-negotiable: a defined severity/duration threshold triggers an explicit, unskippable referral card ('this needs a physio — here are three in Damansara') so the product never drifts into clinical claims.

---

### [5/5 · medium] The extrinsic-to-intrinsic transition is unmanaged everywhere. Every points system in this market scales UP forever, when the evidence says the points are scaffolding that must be deliberately taken down — and Malaysia has the clearest possible proof of what happens when it isn't.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** Deci, Koestner & Ryan (1999): expected tangible rewards undermine intrinsic motivation for the rewarded activity. Charness & Gneezy (Econometrica, 2009): paying people to attend the gym created LASTING habit specifically for the previously-sedentary — the payment worked by buying enough repetitions for a habit to form, i.e. it functioned as scaffolding with a natural end. Gneezy & Rustichini's Israeli daycare fine study (Journal of Legal Studies, 2000) is the canonical demonstration that once you price a behaviour you cannot easily un-price it. Malaysia supplies the field evidence: the researchers observe of BookDoc's challenge machinery that 'engagement collapses when the corporate challenge window closes', that Activ@Work is 'campaign-shaped: intense during competition windows, dormant otherwise', and that WeWard's reward rate 'reads as insulting once users do the maths'. The whole national incentive layer teaches transactional participation and then proves it doesn't survive the payout stopping.

**Who fails it:** AIA Vitality (RM10 cashback — and it sets a brutal reference price: the researchers note any standalone app is competing against something that is net cash-POSITIVE to the user). Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern Rewards, Fave (voucher-hopping, which 'trains deal-hopping, not gym loyalty'). JomRun (event-driven, 'dead between races'). And Hybrid Combative HUD's own XP economy if it just keeps inflating.

**Hybrid play:** Design an explicit three-phase arc and TELL THE ATHLETE it is happening — announcing the fade is what stops it reading as the app getting worse. PHASE 1 (weeks 0-8, scaffolding): dense XP, loud per-rep feedback, every session pays. PHASE 2 (weeks 8-24, handover): XP visibly fades; feedback becomes purely informational and self-referential; the app starts ASKING instead of AWARDING ('your jab speed drops in round 4 — gas or focus?'). PHASE 3 (24+ weeks, contribution): the athlete gets a ROLE — they can author a bounty for a newer member, their round becomes the reference clip for a technique in the Technique Ledger, they mentor a fire team. Contribution and status inside a real physical community is the most durable intrinsic reward a single gym can manufacture, it costs almost nothing to build, and it is structurally unavailable to ClassPass, AIA or any chain. Deliberately deprecating your own gamification is the boldest move on this list and the most defensible.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Ramadan is treated everywhere as a nutrition mode. It is actually a training-protocol problem — and no app on earth changes how a combat athlete TRAINS for those 30 days.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** Across the whole Malaysian market the research found exactly two products with any Ramadan awareness: MalaysianCalorie (sahur/iftar times by location plus a bazaar guide) and JomRun (a 'Fit Ramadhan' virtual run). Both are nutrition/event features. What actually changes for training is documented in local coverage (The Rakyat Post 'Ramadan reps', Buro247): volume drops to roughly 3-4 sessions/week at 30-45 minutes, and sessions migrate to either the ~60 minutes before Maghrib (fat-loss intent) or the post-terawih 21:30-00:00 window (muscle-building intent). Hydration is confined to the iftar-to-sahur window. The research is blunt that a streak system penalising a fasting Muslim for missing daytime sessions is 'culturally broken'. Timing: Ramadan 1448 falls around mid-February 2027 (APPROXIMATE — verify the Hijri date), giving roughly six months of runway, and it recurs annually forever.

**Who fails it:** Every global app (Strava, MyFitnessPal, Apple Fitness+, Samsung Health), every Malaysian chain app (Fitness First, Celebrity Fitness, Anytime Fitness), every studio backend (Vibefam, Mindbody, ClassPass), and every incentive scheme (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ) — all of which keep counting daily streaks and step targets straight through a month when most of the country cannot train in daylight or drink water.

**Hybrid play:** A PUASA PROTOCOL state the whole app enters on the Hijri calendar. (a) Re-normalise the XP curve so a 35-minute post-terawih session pays what a 75-minute normal-month session pays — the athlete is graded against the fasted-month baseline, not punished by it. (b) Rest shields auto-issue for the fasting window instead of being spent. (c) The streak counts weeks, not days, for 30 days. (d) /home-base caps Shadow Dojo rounds and swaps the 'Fatigue vs Failure' prompt for a fasted-state check (dizzy / cramping / dry mouth → end the session, never 'push'). (e) The session planner anchors blocks to Maghrib and Isyak computed on-device from lat/long (JAKIM e-Solat zone SGR01 covers Petaling/Damansara, or use a local astronomical calculation — no network needed). Ship it with visible 'RAMADAN OPS' brutalist chrome: it is the single most legible signal that this app was built here and not ported.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Halal is pointed at the food photo, where it is hardest and most dangerous to answer. The halal question a GYM actually generates is about whey, gelatin capsules, creatine, pre-workout and BCAA — packaged, certified, barcoded products — and no fitness app touches supplements at all.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** The research is explicit that halal status must come from the JAKIM registry (Verify Halal / Serunai Commerce, covering products, brands, premises and abattoirs, with MOUs across 30+ foreign certification bodies) and that 'an AI vision model must NEVER assert halal status from a photo' — a credibility and religious-offence risk. MalaysianCalorie's 'JAKIM Watch' is a reference guide, not certified data. Most Malaysian eating happens at uncertified mamak, hawker and kopitiam stalls where no registry can help, so food-side halal is largely unanswerable — while the anxiety genuinely concentrates on packaged goods and supplements, where gelatin and alcohol-derived flavourings are the recurring issues. Verify Halal is free, authoritative, and has zero nutrition data and zero habit loop.

**Who fails it:** Verify Halal (correct data, no fitness context, utility app with no reason to return), MalaysianCalorie (guide, not certified data), every gym chain app, every supplement retailer app, and every global tracker (no concept of halal whatsoever).

**Hybrid play:** Point the existing /scanner OCR pipeline at the supplement tub instead of the treadmill console. Photograph the label → extract brand, product, JAKIM/MS1500 certification marking and number, and the full ingredient panel → surface three separately-labelled, honestly-scoped facts: (1) 'CERT MARKING DETECTED: [number] — tap to verify in Verify Halal' as a deep link, never an assertion; (2) 'INGREDIENTS FLAGGED FOR REVIEW: gelatin (capsule shell), natural flavouring'; (3) macros per scoop auto-added to /fuel. Hard rule enforced in both the prompt and the UI: the model reports what is PRINTED and never infers status. This is a trust artefact in a Muslim-majority gym, it attaches directly to the gym's own supplement shelf (a revenue line the owner cares about), and literally nobody ships it.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Body-composition thresholds are Western. Malaysian clinical practice uses Asian cutoffs, and Malay and Indian-Malaysian populations carry higher T2D and cardiovascular risk at LOWER BMI — so a member sitting at BMI 24 is told he is fine by every app he owns while local guidance would already flag him.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** FLAG — the specific figures here come from general knowledge, not this research corpus, and must be verified before shipping: WHO Asia-Pacific / Malaysian CPG obesity cutoffs of approximately 23 (overweight) and 27.5 (obese) versus the global 25/30; NHMS 2023 reporting roughly 1 in 6 Malaysian adults with diabetes and over half overweight or obese. What the corpus DOES establish is that the entire Malaysian health-tech layer is built around exactly these NCDs — Naluri (~US$19.9M raised) targets obesity, diabetes, hypertension and cholesterol; Pulse and BookDoc sit on the same disease set — which confirms the risk framing is locally credible. But Naluri's framing is clinical and sedentary (disease reversal), the exact opposite of a combat-gym progression identity, and it has no training telemetry at all.

**Who fails it:** MyFitnessPal, Cal AI, Samsung Health, Apple Fitness+ and every chain app compute against Western cutoffs; Fitness First's Boditrax produces body-composition data that stays trapped in-club; and the local disease-management players (Naluri, ThoughtFull, Pulse) have credible risk framing but no training layer to attach it to.

**Hybrid play:** Add a seventh axis to the /hud radar — METABOLIC — computed from waist-to-height ratio (≥0.5 as the flag), resting heart rate, and weekly training minutes, scored against Asian cutoffs BY DEFAULT with the reason printed on the card. Waist is tape-measured with a camera framing overlay showing where the tape sits. The camera-only unlock is resting HR by PPG: fingertip over the rear camera with the torch on, ~30 seconds of frame-averaged red-channel signal. That is a real vital sign, on-device, from the only sensor the gym has, and it converts 'I trained hard this month' into 'my resting HR went from 74 to 66' — the single most motivating number available to an athlete with no wearable. Present strictly as trend telemetry, never as diagnosis, and route anything alarming to the free screening bounty below.

---

### [5/5 · small] Malaysia gives adults free government health screening, and not one fitness app converts a motivated gym member into a screened one — or lets them bring the numbers back into the app.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** MySejahtera now carries National Health Screening Initiative records from government clinics free of charge, alongside a Health Card Wallet and myMinda entry points — and the research found NO fitness, step or workout features in it whatsoever, despite its near-universal install base. Screening records only flow from Klinik Kesihatan touchpoints; private gym and clinic data is invisible to it. MOH also runs Jom Saring and the KOSPEN community screening programme. The monetary framing matters: a private teleconsult is RM39.99 (DoctorOnCall) and home physiotherapy is RM250/session (Homage), so the free government path has real, quotable value.

**Who fails it:** MySejahtera (authoritative records, zero motivation loop), every private fitness app (motivation loop, zero records), Naluri and BookDoc (employer- or insurer-gated, so an independent gym member cannot reach them), and AIA/Great Eastern (policyholder-gated).

**Hybrid play:** A BLOODWORK bounty. At level milestones the HUD issues a rare, high-XP quest: 'Get screened at Klinik Kesihatan [nearest] — free. Bring back the slip.' The member photographs the printed slip; Claude vision OCRs HbA1c, fasting glucose, total/HDL/LDL cholesterol and blood pressure into structured fields; the METABOLIC axis then moves on real clinical numbers, and the app tracks the delta at the next screening. The vision task is EASY compared to nasi campur — a printed lab slip with fixed layout and high contrast — and the payoff is a solo-dev app doing something the ~US$20M-funded incumbents do not: closing the loop between free public healthcare and private training motivation. Massive credibility with the gym owner and with older members.

---

### [5/5 · large] Combat and strength athletes are invisible to every reward scheme in Malaysia, because all of them pay on steps or on auto-captured device data. A 90-minute Muay Thai session scores approximately zero nationwide.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** Two independent researchers reached this conclusion separately. Every major behavioural incentive in the country — AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, MOH's KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern — is step-count based. Critically, AIA Vitality awards points ONLY for genuine workout data automatically captured by a linked fitness app or device; manually added workout data earns nothing and syncing takes up to 48 hours. AIA pays RM10 cashback for hitting the weekly target, 100 points per gym day, and RM100/200/300 tiered gym vouchers by status — which is also the RM10/month price anchor the entire category is judged against. Note also that BookDoc, a doctor-booking app, is the rail powering BOTH national step programmes.

**Who fails it:** AIA Vitality, Great Eastern Rewards, BookDoc / Activ@Work / KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard — and the gym itself, which currently has no mechanism to make its members' training count anywhere outside its own four walls.

**Hybrid play:** Make the HUD an EMITTER, not just a tracker. A Shadow Dojo session (pose-verified, rep-counted, timestamped) or a scanner-verified cardio console session is precisely the kind of sensor-derived record these schemes want — write it to Health Connect as a completed exercise session so AIA reads it as auto-captured, and sparring rounds turn into real cashback. Two honest caveats to state up front: a PWA cannot write to Health Connect, so this needs a thin TWA wrapper (Bubblewrap) plus a small native bridge — the one genuinely non-trivial build on this list; and AIA's acceptance of arbitrary Health Connect sources MUST be verified before any cashback promise appears in copy. But it is the only feature here that makes the app PAY the member, which is exactly the expectation AIA has set at RM10/month — and no competitor can follow, because none of them can verify that a session happened in the first place.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Every pose-based product markets absolute joint angles, 'form scores' and left/right symmetry — which is precisely the class of measurement single-camera pose does WORST. The reliable class (time, frequency, velocity) is the one nobody markets.

**Lens:** HARDWARE-FREE SENSING — how close is WHOOP-class readiness with zero hardware?

BOTTOM LINE: A phone gets you roughly 70% of WHOOP's readiness stack, and 0% of the 30% that constitutes WHOOP's actual moat. Chasing the remaining 30% is the trap. Inverting the axis is the win.

WHAT IS GENUINELY WHOOP-CLASS TODAY:
• Heart rate via CONTACT fingertip PPG (finger over rear lens + torch on): 1.6% ± 4.3% MAPE vs reference in 95 subjects (Google Health, Communications Medicine 2022). Critically, there was NO skin-tone gradient — 1.8% (very light–intermediate), 1.3% (tan/brown), 1.8% (dark) — because the torch transilluminates the finger rather than reflecting off melanin. For a Malaysian gym this is decisive.
• Respiratory rate from front camera, subject sitting still: MAE 0.78 ± 0.61 breaths/min (n=50), holding at 0.80 ± 0.60 in people with chronic respiratory disease. WHOOP charges money for this number.
• Resting HR trend: trivially reachable, and one of the more robust illness/overreaching signals in existence.

WHAT IS NOT:
• HRV. A camera-PPG app produced 17.49% MAPE on RMSSD against 2.16% for a Polar chest strap in 37 trained athletes (Johansson et al., Frontiers in Physiology 2025). Day-to-day biological variation in lnRMSSD is only ~3.1–5.8% (Flatt 2019). So a SINGLE camera HRV reading is mostly measurement noise — it becomes signal only as a 7-day rolling mean (√7 ≈ 2.6× noise reduction). Frequency-domain HRV (LF/HF) is unusable: it destabilises at 10% data degradation while median IBI survives to 60%.
• FACIAL rPPG in a gym. Three independent failure modes stack exactly where Hybrid operates: melanin (respiratory error 0.45 bpm light-skinned vs 0.80 dark-skinned; Fitzpatrick VI carried OR 5.08 for measurement failure in a BP study), elevated heart rate (5 of 8 rPPG methods degraded significantly at high HR — and notably low illumination mattered LESS than high HR, per the CHILL dataset, npj Digital Medicine 2025), and motion/sweat. The pretty demo (point front camera at face between rounds) is the single worst engineering choice available. Use the finger and the torch.
• Sleep staging and continuous in-session HR. Structurally unreachable — both require a device on the body while the user is not holding one. Do not fake these.

THE INVERSION — the signature that runs through the entire pose literature:
Cameras measure TIME, FREQUENCY and VELOCITY well. They measure MAGNITUDE, ABSOLUTE POSITION and DEPTH badly. Evidence is remarkably consistent: tremor peak frequency was accurate while tremor amplitude was "insufficient for clinical application"; markerless boxing showed elbow position errors >3 cm while segment velocities showed "good-to-excellent agreement"; MediaPipe knee flexion MAE 3.15–4.10° but ankle "poor concordance"; even two-camera MediaPipe 3D RMSE ranged 7.88 mm (knee medial-lateral) to 54.55 mm (ankle anterior-posterior); gait symmetry was the single weakest derived metric ("moderate to weak"). Single-camera z-depth is not usable, full stop.

That signature points at exactly one readiness marker that is combat-specific AND better on a phone than on any wrist strap: PUNCH VELOCITY DECAY. Fatigue significantly reduced punch velocity and impact force in MMA fighters (p<0.01), and 2D video agreed with 3D infrared motion capture at r = 0.964–0.999, ICC 0.81–0.99. WHOOP cannot see this. Neither can any Malaysian competitor — no Malaysian app does movement CV at all.

THE BIGGEST WIN IS NOT A SENSOR:
Lab-condition MediaPipe rep counting hits 97.2% accuracy (MAE 0.06 reps). The same task with real users placing their own phones hits 61.1% for push-ups and 61.5% for squats (JMIR mHealth 2026, n=44). Within that same study, correct diagonal framing at 1.8–2.0 m recovered 85.7–95.5% (MAE 0.05–0.28), while side-on at 90 cm gave 0% detection and front-on at 360 cm gave 20%. A 36-point accuracy gap is sitting in camera-placement UX, not in the model. This is the highest-ROI engineering work available to a solo dev, and it costs a reticle and a distance check.

VERDICT ON "WHOOP-CLASS READINESS WITH ZERO HARDWARE": Reachable for heart rate, respiratory rate and resting-HR trend. Reachable-with-caveats for HRV if and only if you ship 7-day rolling means and refuse to display daily numbers. Unreachable for sleep and continuous strain. But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** The pattern is consistent across independent studies. Tremor analysis: MediaPipe accurately measured peak FREQUENCY, but 'accuracy of amplitude estimation compared to OMC was insufficient for clinical application' (Scientific Reports 2025). Markerless boxing vs 12-camera optoelectronic: elbow position differences >3 cm, shoulder/wrist <2.5 cm, but 'segment velocities displayed good-to-excellent agreement' (Frontiers in Sports and Active Living 2022). Single-camera smartphone MediaPipe: knee flexion MAE 4.10° ± 2.32° and 3.15° ± 3.10°, ROM MAE 4.55°/4.15°, correlations 0.845–0.916 — but ankle showed 'poor concordance' with significant systematic errors (Sensors 2026). Gait: sagittal CCC ≥ 0.624 and frontal CCC ≥ 0.805 for cadence/step time/stance time — but 'only for gait symmetry... moderate to weak correlation' (Sensors 2025). Depth: even TWO-camera MediaPipe gave RMSE 7.88 mm (knee medial-lateral) to 54.55 mm (ankle anterior-posterior), with bias up to +53.47 mm. MediaPipe 2D reliability ICC 0.85 vs 0.92 for a stereo rig.

**Who fails it:** Every AI form-check app claims joint-angle precision it cannot support. Kemtai, Exer, Onform, and the entire 'AI personal trainer' category. In Malaysia, Joompa claims AI 'creates personalized programs' with no described mechanism; Vibefam markets itself as 'AI-powered' for what is CRM automation. The bar for credibility is on the floor.

**Hybrid play:** Re-architect Shadow Dojo scoring around the reliable class: rep TEMPO (eccentric/concentric split), CADENCE, rhythm CONSISTENCY (coefficient of variation across reps), and intra-set VELOCITY DECAY. Demote absolute joint angles to advisory with a stated ±4° uncertainty, and demote symmetry to 'indicative only' or remove it. Ship a visible per-metric CONFIDENCE TIER in the HUD (A: timing/frequency — validated; B: 2D sagittal angles — ±4°; C: depth/symmetry — not measurable single-camera). In a market where every AI claim is marketing, publishing your own error bars is the most defensible possible position — and it fits 'tactical telemetry' better than a fake 92% form score ever will.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Punch velocity decay is a validated, combat-specific fatigue marker that 2D phone video measures at near-3D-motion-capture agreement. It is the only readiness signal that is BETTER on a phone than on a WHOOP — and no product in the world ships it.

**Lens:** HARDWARE-FREE SENSING — how close is WHOOP-class readiness with zero hardware?

Neither can any Malaysian competitor — no Malaysian app does movement CV at all.

THE BIGGEST WIN IS NOT A SENSOR:

The same task with real users placing their own phones hits 61.1% for push-ups and 61.5% for squats (JMIR mHealth 2026, n=44). Reachable-with-caveats for HRV if and only if you ship 7-day rolling means and refuse to display daily numbers. Unreachable for sleep and continuous strain. But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** MMA study (Sensors 2025, n=21 male fighters, mean age 24.85 ± 7.24): 'Fatigue significantly decreased punch velocity and impact force (p < 0.01)', and critically the 2D system (Dartfish) agreed with 3D infrared motion capture at r = 0.964–0.999 with ICC 0.81–0.99. Markerless boxing analysis confirms segment velocities show 'good-to-excellent agreement' with optoelectronic reference even where absolute positions do not (Frontiers in Sports and Active Living 2022). Inertial boxing systems validated against Qualisys reached velocity r = 0.785–0.854, ICC 0.883–0.950, CV 2.27–7.67% (Frontiers in Physiology 2026) — i.e. the phone-camera route is competitive with dedicated sensors. Professional boxers show 22–31% higher punch acceleration than amateurs (p<0.05), so the metric also discriminates skill level.

**Who fails it:** WHOOP, Oura, Garmin — structurally blind to movement, they see only heart rate and steps. Every Malaysian incentive scheme (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard) is step-count based, meaning 200 rounds on the bag scores near zero. TRIBE Fitness, Monarchy MMA, Panthera Muay Thai and Team Thoo run Vibefam or Mindbody, which track bookings and at best belt rank. Not one tracks what the athlete actually did.

**Hybrid play:** 'VELOCITY FLOOR' — a 10-second, 10-punch max-intent cross protocol at session start and at session end, from the same tripod-free wall-lean camera position. Report the intra-session percentage drop as the primary fatigue readout, and the session-start absolute as the readiness readout against a 7-day baseline. Make THIS the headline readiness metric on /hud instead of HRV — it is more valid, more combat-specific, more visible to the athlete, and it feeds the 6-axis stat radar with something no competitor can compute. Report velocity in normalised units (relative to the athlete's own rolling max), which sidesteps the camera's absolute-scale weakness entirely.

---

### [5/5 · medium] Heat strain is the most Malaysia-specific physiological signal in existence — 80%+ humidity year-round, indoor training forced by heat and haze — and not one fitness product on earth ships heat-strain readiness for gym athletes.

**Lens:** HARDWARE-FREE SENSING — how close is WHOOP-class readiness with zero hardware?

BOTTOM LINE: A phone gets you roughly 70% of WHOOP's readiness stack, and 0% of the 30% that constitutes WHOOP's actual moat. Chasing the remaining 30% is the trap. Inverting the axis is the win.

Critically, there was NO skin-tone gradient — 1.8% (very light–intermediate), 1.3% (tan/brown), 1.8% (dark) — because the torch transilluminates the finger rather than reflecting off melanin. For a Malaysian gym this is decisive.
• Respiratory rate from front camera, subject sitting still: MAE 0.78 ± 0.61 breaths/min (n=50), holding at 0.80 ± 0.60 in people with chronic respiratory disease. WHOOP charges money for this number.
• Resting HR trend: trivially reachable, and one of the more robust illness/overreaching signals in existence.

• HRV. A camera-PPG app produced 17.49% MAPE on RMSSD against 2.16% for a Polar chest strap in 37 trained athletes (Johansson et al., Frontiers in Physiology 2025). Frequency-domain HRV (LF/HF) is unusable: it destabilises at 10% data degradation while median IBI survives to 60%.
• FACIAL rPPG in a gym. The pretty demo (point front camera at face between rounds) is the single worst engineering choice available. Use the finger and the torch.
• Sleep staging and continuous in-session HR. Structurally unreachable — both require a device on the body while the user is not holding one. Do not fake these.

THE INVERSION — the signature that runs through the entire pose literature:
Cameras measure TIME, FREQUENCY and VELOCITY well. They measure MAGNITUDE, ABSOLUTE POSITION and DEPTH badly. Evidence is remarkably consistent: tremor peak frequency was accurate while tremor amplitude was "insufficient for clinical application"; markerless boxing showed elbow position errors >3 cm while segment velocities showed "good-to-excellent agreement"; MediaPipe knee flexion MAE 3.15–4.10° but ankle "poor concordance"; even two-camera MediaPipe 3D RMSE ranged 7.88 mm (knee medial-lateral) to 54.55 mm (ankle anterior-posterior); gait symmetry was the single weakest derived metric ("moderate to weak"). Single-camera z-depth is not usable, full stop.

That signature points at exactly one readiness marker that is combat-specific AND better on a phone than on any wrist strap: PUNCH VELOCITY DECAY. Fatigue significantly reduced punch velocity and impact force in MMA fighters (p<0.01), and 2D video agreed with 3D infrared motion capture at r = 0.964–0.999, ICC 0.81–0.99. WHOOP cannot see this. Neither can any Malaysian competitor — no Malaysian app does movement CV at all.

THE BIGGEST WIN IS NOT A SENSOR:
Lab-condition MediaPipe rep counting hits 97.2% accuracy (MAE 0.06 reps). The same task with real users placing their own phones hits 61.1% for push-ups and 61.5% for squats (JMIR mHealth 2026, n=44). Within that same study, correct diagonal framing at 1.8–2.0 m recovered 85.7–95.5% (MAE 0.05–0.28), while side-on at 90 cm gave 0% detection and front-on at 360 cm gave 20%. A 36-point accuracy gap is sitting in camera-placement UX, not in the model. This is the highest-ROI engineering work available to a solo dev, and it costs a reticle and a distance check.

Reachable-with-caveats for HRV if and only if you ship 7-day rolling means and refuse to display daily numbers. Unreachable for sleep and continuous strain. But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** Core body temperature is estimable from a heart-rate sequence alone: the ECTemp algorithm validated in 102 outdoor construction workers gave bias -0.16 ± 0.30 °C (Polar input) and -0.09 ± 0.28 °C (Garmin input) across a full summer work shift (Environmental Research Communications 2026). That accuracy is genuinely useful — but it requires CONTINUOUS heart rate, which a camera cannot supply during training. The honest hardware-free substitute is heart rate recovery, which does discriminate training status: elite vs recreational judoka showed differential HRR responses of -5.6 ± 3.3% vs -9.8 ± 4.7% post Special Judo Fitness Test (Scientific Reports 2026). Standardisation is the binding constraint — the literature repeatedly notes HRR utility is context-dependent and that measurement timing (1 min, 15 min post) must be fixed.

**Who fails it:** WHOOP and Garmin report 'heat acclimation' from continuous wrist HR but nothing gym-specific or humidity-aware. Every Malaysian app ignores climate entirely despite it being the dominant environmental fact of training there. Strava and WeWard are structurally weak for most of the year because heat, monsoon and haze push training indoors. Nobody has connected humidity to readiness.

**Hybrid play:** 'HEAT DEBT' — a 30-second fingertip PPG capture at a FIXED interval after a fixed round (e.g. exactly 60 s after round 3), giving HRR60 against the athlete's own rolling baseline. Track the within-session slope: HRR60 collapsing across rounds is a defensible heat-strain and fatigue signal that needs no continuous stream. Overlay local temperature/humidity. Do NOT claim to estimate core temperature — state plainly that continuous HR is required for that and you do not have it. Ship it as a coach-facing amber flag ('HEAT DEBT RISING — SHORTEN ROUNDS') rather than a medical claim. Being the only product in the world that treats Malaysian humidity as a first-class training variable is a strong, honest, defensible position.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Coach-in-the-loop inverts the AI accuracy economics that kill every global app — a wrong estimate at one gym is a labelled training example, not a churned user

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** The NIH/ASN 2026 study of 102 nutrient-controlled meals found all four leading photo-calorie apps underestimated energy by ~33%: Cal AI −345 kcal, Lose It! −333, MyFitnessPal −327, Appediet −252. HealthifyMe's own reported Snap accuracy is 60–70% (≈75% with human-hybrid) on its *home* cuisine. Samsung Health's on-device food model benchmarks at 64.1% ID rate with ±26% portion error. A global app has no mechanism to recover from these errors — the user just stops trusting it. A single gym has a coach who sees the member twice a week.

**Who fails it:** Cal AI, MyFitnessPal, HealthifyMe Snap, Berry Best, MalaysianCalorie, MyDietPRO — all ship a confident number with no correction loop and no one accountable for it. Their marketed accuracy (Cal AI advertises 92–97%) is measured as the worst of four.

**Hybrid play:** Make uncertainty a first-class UI object across /fuel, /scanner and /home-base: every inference ships with a confidence band and a one-tap 'flag for coach'. Build a Coach Review queue — the coach clears 10 flagged items in 90 seconds between rounds, and each correction writes to a gym-local ground-truth table. Show the member 'Coach Faiz corrected this' — which is simultaneously the accuracy fix, the retention moment, and the data moat. After a year the gym owns a human-verified dataset of its own members' food, machines and movement that no funded competitor can buy.

---

### [4/5 · small] A known equipment inventory turns console OCR and pose from a research problem into a configuration problem — global apps must generalise over every machine on earth, this gym has eleven

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Research flags Malaysian gyms running mixed fleets (Life Fitness, Technogym, Matrix, Precor, cheap local imports) with LED-segment displays that behave badly under camera rolling shutter, and concludes 'expect to need per-console calibration'. For a global app, per-console calibration is an impossible support burden. For one gym it is an afternoon with a label printer. Hapana's floor-level equivalent is only 'on-floor check-in for equipment and spot tracking' — presence, not readings.

**Who fails it:** Every OCR/vision fitness app that must work anywhere. Also every management platform: PushPress, Hapana and Vibefam know a member checked in, none know what the treadmill said.

**Hybrid play:** Ship a Machine Registry: a QR/NFC sticker on each console keyed to a stored crop region, digit template, glare profile and torch requirement, calibrated once by the owner. Scanning becomes near-deterministic instead of best-effort. Two free consequences worth more than the OCR: per-machine leaderboards ('Assault Bike #2, all-time top 10, this gym only'), and a maintenance signal for the owner — 'Rower 1 scans down 62% this month' usually means it is broken and nobody told you.

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Gym-floor reality per the research: high humidity makes screens mis-register touch, basements have no signal, and 'no long text entry mid-set' is a hard constraint. Every global app opens with 'what are you doing today?'. Meanwhile AIA Vitality — Malaysia's largest reward loop — awards points ONLY for workout data automatically captured by a linked app or device and explicitly nothing for manually added workouts. Manual entry is both a UX tax and a disqualification.

**Who fails it:** Strong, Hevy, MyFitnessPal, Samsung Health, Strava — none can know a class exists. ClassPass knows a booking exists but nothing about what happened in the room. Vibefam holds the timetable but never uses it to pre-arm a member's session.

**Hybrid play:** Pre-arm the HUD from the timetable: walk in at 19:52 on a Tuesday and the app already shows 'MUAY THAI · COACH FAIZ · ROUND 1 READY' with zero taps. Fire a geofence/BLE-beacon or QR-at-the-door check-in that both marks attendance and opens the correct session template. Then emit the finished session into Health Connect / Google Fit as an auto-captured workout so members' gym training converts into AIA Vitality points and RM10 weekly cashback — verify AIA's acceptance criteria before promising it, but if it holds, the app literally pays for the membership.

### [4/5 · medium] Nobody reports retention attributed to coach, time slot and class type — every platform reports attendance, which is the input, not the outcome

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** PushPress ships 'attendance trending and class performance reports'; Hapana ships 15–50+ operational reports by tier; both are volume reporting. Vibefam's reporting is revenue-ops shaped. The research's own verdict on the whole category is blunt: martial arts studios' software 'tracks bookings, attendance and at best belt rank. Not one of them tracks what the athlete actually did.' Attendance tells an owner a class was full; it never tells them the 7am class produces members who are still there in nine months while the packed Friday 8pm class is a revolving door.

**Who fails it:** PushPress, Glofox, Mindbody, Hapana, Zen Planner, Vibefam — all report attendance and revenue by class; none attribute 90-day and 12-month survival to the coach who ran the session.

**Hybrid play:** Cohort every member by first-30-days experience and run survival curves sliced by coach, time slot, class type and first-session intensity. Deliver four numbers a month: 90-day survival by coach, trial→member conversion by first class attended, sessions/week/member (the strongest known retention predictor) and pack burn rate vs expiry. This is the board that justifies firing a class, moving a slot, or paying a coach more — and it is the single most saleable artefact in the whole product when you take it to a second gym.

### [4/5 · small] Belt and rank in every platform is metadata a coach declares; nobody has made grading evidence-backed — which is exactly the artefact that makes a rank non-portable

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Vibefam's martial-arts differentiation is 'belt-rank class scheduling' — scheduling students by rank, i.e. rank as a filter field. The research's summary of the category: rank 'records what a coach declares, never what the athlete did.' Combat-sport grading in Klang Valley is subjective and undocumented, which makes it low-status relative to BJJ/IBJJF-style federated ranks and makes a member's rank at one gym worthless when they leave — or, phrased the other way, means nothing currently anchors them.

**Who fails it:** Vibefam, Zen Planner (US martial-arts belt tracking is still coach-entered — flag: I could not fetch Zen Planner's page to verify its current feature set), and every consumer app, none of which know combat sport exists.

**Hybrid play:** Generate a Grading Dossier: an auto-compiled PDF/story per candidate showing logged rounds, sparring sessions, technique-specific form scores over time, attendance streaks, corrective-drill compliance and the coach's own recorded notes. The coach still decides — the dossier just makes the decision defensible and the rank feel earned. Two effects: it turns a subjective ceremony into a keepsake members photograph and post, and it makes the rank *documented by this gym*, which is the most honest form of switching cost an independent gym can build against ClassPass and the cheaper gym down the road.

### [4/5 · small] Retention is caused by a human noticing you, not by a notification — so the software's job is to tell the coach who to notice, and then get out of the way via WhatsApp

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** The research is unambiguous that 'WhatsApp is the operating system of Malaysian life, including gym life: class confirmations, PT check-ins, coach feedback, gym announcements and payment screenshots all happen in WhatsApp groups. Any product that fights WhatsApp loses.' It also notes AIA Vitality's rewards and Celebrity Fitness's check-in badges are 'attendance theatre'. WeWard's economics (≈100 days of 10k steps for USD 10) show digital reward currency reads as insulting once users do the arithmetic. What a global app structurally cannot do is have someone the member respects notice their absence.

**Who fails it:** Every push-notification retention engine: Celebrity Fitness badges, AIA Vitality points, WeWard Wards, ClassPass reminders. PushPress and Hapana automate email/SMS from the *brand*, which reads as marketing.

**Hybrid play:** Never let the app send the retention message. Build a Coach Console that drafts it and hands it over: one tap opens WhatsApp pre-filled — 'Aiman, teep form dropped 18% and you've not logged since Tuesday — everything ok with that knee?' — sent from the coach's own number. Add coach-granted rest shields: only the coach can issue injury, travel and Raya shields, which makes the coach the dispenser of grace rather than the app the dispenser of guilt. And make the shareable output a WhatsApp-shaped card (portrait, legible at thumbnail, gym-branded) alongside the existing Instagram story — WhatsApp reaches the uncles, Instagram reaches the 24-year-olds.
