### [4/5 · medium] The Malaysian training calendar produces four predictable, dated churn cliffs a year — and a global app will never build for any of them

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Research: only MalaysianCalorie (Ramadan mode with location-derived sahur/iftar times) and JomRun ('Fit Ramadhan' virtual run) show any Ramadan awareness at all; everything else 'ignores a month that upends training for the majority of the population'. Concrete behavioural shifts documented: sessions move to ~1hr pre-iftar or 9:30pm–midnight post-terawih, volume drops to 3–4 sessions/week at 30–45 minutes, hydration compresses to the iftar→sahur window. Hari Raya adds 'a week-plus of travel back to kampung and heavy eating', and CNY, Deepavali and school holidays produce further predictable adherence collapses. A streak system that punishes a fasting member is culturally broken.

**Who fails it:** Naluri (US$20M raised, no Bahasa Melayu website, no Ramadan protocol), Apple Fitness+, Strava, MyFitnessPal, ClassPass, and every gym-management platform — none of which reshape programming, streaks or scheduling around the local calendar.

**Hybrid play:** Ship Calendar Modes as first-class product state, not settings. RAMADAN MODE: rest shields auto-apply to daytime, XP curve rescaled to a 3–4 session week, /fuel switches to a sahur/iftar eating window instead of a generic 16:8 timer, class slots and reminders shift to pre-iftar and post-terawih. KAMPUNG MODE: the two weeks after Raya, the Shadow Dojo becomes a no-equipment home programme with a 'kampung ladder' so members compete from their hometowns and return with their streak intact — this is the highest-leverage single churn intervention in the Malaysian year and it uses the /home-base engine you already built. Same shape for CNY and Deepavali.

### [4/5 · small] The gym can see the social graph on the floor — who trains with whom, who brought whom — which is both the strongest known retention lever and completely invisible to any remote platform

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Combat gyms are referral-driven and pair-based by construction: pad rounds, drilling and sparring all require a partner, so the training-partner graph is physically observable and gets recorded automatically the moment two members check into the same session repeatedly. No platform captures it: PushPress tracks check-ins per member, Vibefam tracks bookings, ClassPass explicitly destroys continuity ('no member history persists for the gym — the gym cannot see who is progressing'). Meanwhile the research's read on why Fittr works is community and peer accountability, not software — and notes bluntly that a community moat 'is irrelevant to a single-gym product — the gym IS the community, already physically present.'

**Who fails it:** Every management platform (graph not modelled), every consumer app (no co-presence data), ClassPass (structurally anti-continuity).

**Hybrid play:** Derive a training-partner graph from co-attendance and Shadow Dojo pairing, then use it two ways. Retention: surface members with zero training partners to the coach — they are the churn risk nobody sees, and the fix is a five-second pairing decision at the start of class, not a discount. Growth: surface the referral tree so the owner can see that four members trace back to one person, and reward that person with rank/status/physical perks rather than a voucher. Add a 'Partner Streak' — two members' streaks bound together, so missing a session lets down a named human rather than a progress bar. That is a mechanic no remote app can even represent.

### [4/5 · medium] White-label is a trap if sold as software and a moat if sold as an operating outcome — and the correct second customer is a rival gym on the ladder, not a stranger on a landing page

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** The evidence against a naive white-label SaaS play is overwhelming: KFit's RM99/mo unlimited model failed and pivoted into vouchers; ClassPass re-entered at the same RM99 with credits instead; Naluri (~US$20M raised) and ThoughtFull both route around the consumer wallet entirely and sell to HR; AIA Vitality anchors a 'wellness layer' at RM10/mo *and pays cashback*. Simultaneously, the incumbent local platform Vibefam positions on being cheaper and more local than Mindbody/Glofox and publishes no price — meaning the market is used to negotiated, relationship-led, MYR-denominated deals, not self-serve SKUs. A solo dev cannot win a self-serve SaaS motion; a solo dev embedded in a gym can win a referral motion inside a rivalry network.

**Who fails it:** Every VC-funded platform's go-to-market: PushPress and Hapana need self-serve or inside sales at US price points; Glofox needs a quote call; ClassPass needs a marketplace. None of them can be sold by a coach to a rival coach after an interclub.

**Hybrid play:** Sequence it deliberately. (1) Prove it at Hybrid Combative with a holdout cohort and publish one number: MYR of membership saved per month. (2) Onboard ladder rivals at RM250–450/gym/month with a 90-day 'we install your Machine Registry and your Canteen for you' concierge onboarding — the setup work IS the moat, because it is physical and per-site. (3) Add a corporate package: an inter-office combat challenge scored on real session data rather than steps, sold to HR — the only Malaysian wallet that reliably converts — and priced per headcount. Never sell booking, billing or a member subscription. Explicitly commit to member data export so the switching cost is social and physical, not hostage-taking; the rank, the partners, the coach and the wall are what do not travel.

---

### [4/5 · small] When a member finally sees a physiotherapist they arrive with nothing but a sentence. No product bridges the gym floor to the clinic, and a human coach structurally cannot build that bridge because they do not hold the data.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. Six of the Malaysian nutrition apps and four of the regional ones do food-photo-to-macros — and the NIH/ASN 2026 study measured all four leading photo apps underestimating meal energy by roughly 33% (Cal AI worst at -345 kcal). HealthifyMe's own stated Snap accuracy is 60-75% on its home cuisine. Meanwhile the capabilities that are genuinely superhuman are entirely unclaimed in Malaysia and thinly claimed globally.

A human coach's binding constraints are: (a) they are present for ~3 hours of your week and zero of your meals; (b) their sensing resolution is one pair of eyes at ~10Hz, watching one athlete at a time; (c) their memory is recency-weighted anecdote, not n=400 sessions; (d) they cannot run the future before you live it; (e) their income depends on you, which caps their honesty. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.

Four structural advantages, in order of defensibility for a solo dev with a phone:

1. RESOLUTION. The phone already contains a sensor 1,400x faster than its camera — the microphone — and no fitness product in this corpus uses it. Combat sport is an impact sport; impacts are acoustic transients. 30fps vision literally skips the contact frame of a jab.
2. PRESENCE. Being at every rep AND every meal for the same person for years makes N-of-1 causal inference possible, which is the one thing no dietitian or coach can do. Critically, this does NOT require accurate calories — a systematic bias cancels in a within-subject model. The category is losing an accuracy war it did not need to fight.
3. RECALL + CROSS-REFERENCE. Longitudinal per-joint baselining turns pain from a lagging indicator into a leading one, and turns a gym of 100 bodies into a longitudinal cohort study with known outcomes.
4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. A camera+mic that objectively counts 412 strikes over 47 minutes IS auto-captured sensor data. Hybrid can be the wearable. That is the only route to being net cash-positive for the user, which the RM10/mo AIA anchor and KFit's RM99 failure make mandatory.

Architecture consequence: everything continuous and identity-bearing — pose, audio, PPG — stays on-device, both because mid-range Android on prepaid data cannot afford otherwise and because PDPA-era Malaysians will not send body video offshore. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** Homage Malaysia prices home physiotherapy at RM250/session (corpus). A large fraction of a first session is history-taking that is pure recall guesswork — the physio was in the room for zero of the athlete's 400 sessions and the AI was in the room for all of them. The corpus also flags this as a live credibility risk: 'an app-based corrective drill must be careful never to position itself as a substitute' for real rehab. Feeding the clinician is the posture that resolves the risk instead of running at it.

**Who fails it:** All of them. Homage sells the physio but has no upstream training data. Naluri is talk therapy with no movement layer. Doctor Anywhere and DoctorOnCall are episodic and illness-triggered. Vibefam, Mindbody and Glofox hold bookings. Nothing in this market produces a clinical handover artefact.

**Hybrid play:** One-tap 'Clinic Export' generating a shareable link or PDF: 12 weeks of load history bucketed by movement pattern, the Drift Watch chart for the affected joint, the tap-where-it-hurts timeline, and three auto-selected pose clips (worst rep, first drift session, most recent) — under an explicit 'this is data, not a diagnosis' header, with the member controlling the export (a PDPA trust asset, not just compliance). It makes an RM250 session materially more productive, which is a benefit no competitor can offer and no coach can produce. It also gives Hybrid a legitimate, non-clinical relationship with the physio community in Klang Valley — a referral network a single gym could not otherwise buy.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Malaysia's entire behavioural incentive layer is step-based, so combat and strength athletes score near zero everywhere — and AIA pays only for auto-captured sensor data, which locks out every manual-entry app. Nobody has tried to make the phone itself count as the wearable.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. Six of the Malaysian nutrition apps and four of the regional ones do food-photo-to-macros — and the NIH/ASN 2026 study measured all four leading photo apps underestimating meal energy by roughly 33% (Cal AI worst at -345 kcal). HealthifyMe's own stated Snap accuracy is 60-75% on its home cuisine. Meanwhile the capabilities that are genuinely superhuman are entirely unclaimed in Malaysia and thinly claimed globally.

A human coach's binding constraints are: (a) they are present for ~3 hours of your week and zero of your meals; (b) their sensing resolution is one pair of eyes at ~10Hz, watching one athlete at a time; (c) their memory is recency-weighted anecdote, not n=400 sessions; (d) they cannot run the future before you live it; (e) their income depends on you, which caps their honesty. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.

Four structural advantages, in order of defensibility for a solo dev with a phone:

1. RESOLUTION. The phone already contains a sensor 1,400x faster than its camera — the microphone — and no fitness product in this corpus uses it. Combat sport is an impact sport; impacts are acoustic transients. 30fps vision literally skips the contact frame of a jab.
2. PRESENCE. Being at every rep AND every meal for the same person for years makes N-of-1 causal inference possible, which is the one thing no dietitian or coach can do. Critically, this does NOT require accurate calories — a systematic bias cancels in a within-subject model. The category is losing an accuracy war it did not need to fight.
3. RECALL + CROSS-REFERENCE. Longitudinal per-joint baselining turns pain from a lagging indicator into a leading one, and turns a gym of 100 bodies into a longitudinal cohort study with known outcomes.
4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. A camera+mic that objectively counts 412 strikes over 47 minutes IS auto-captured sensor data. Hybrid can be the wearable. That is the only route to being net cash-positive for the user, which the RM10/mo AIA anchor and KFit's RM99 failure make mandatory.

Architecture consequence: everything continuous and identity-bearing — pose, audio, PPG — stays on-device, both because mid-range Android on prepaid data cannot afford otherwise and because PDPA-era Malaysians will not send body video offshore. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** Corpus, repeatedly: AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, MOH KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard and Great Eastern are all step- or duration-driven; a 90-minute Muay Thai session produces almost no steps. AIA Vitality awards points ONLY for workout data automatically captured by a linked fitness app or device — manually added data earns nothing, sync takes up to 48h. AIA pays RM10 weekly-challenge cashback plus RM100/200/300 tier gym vouchers, and RM10/mo is the price anchor the whole category is measured against. A camera+mic that counts 412 objectively-detected strikes across 47 minutes is auto-captured sensor data by any reasonable reading — it simply is not on a wrist.

**Who fails it:** BookDoc (steps only, and it is the rail under both national programmes). AIA (wearable-dependent). Fitness First's Boditrax (in-club, trapped). Every combat gym in Klang Valley — TRIBE, Monarchy MMA, Panthera, Team Thoo — runs on booking software that emits nothing.

**Hybrid play:** Emit a Health Connect / Google Fit ExerciseSession of type MARTIAL_ARTS with duration and an energy series derived from strike cadence, pose-estimated limb velocity and the between-round HR recovery capture. Then a 'Vitality Bridge' screen showing the member exactly how last month's training converted to ringgit. This would be the only feature in the entire Malaysian market that PAYS a user for doing combat sport, and it neutralises the RM10 price anchor by making Hybrid net cash-positive instead of competing with it. Test honestly and early: AIA may reject third-party-written Health Connect sessions, and that single empirical question determines whether this is a headline feature or a footnote.

---

### [4/5 · medium] The 'no wearable' constraint is treated as a ceiling. The camera is already a cardiovascular sensor — and the metric worth capturing is not heart rate but heart-rate recovery in the 60 seconds after a round, which is dead time anyway.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** Contact photoplethysmography via fingertip on the lens with the torch on is a decade-old, widely-deployed smartphone technique, and heart-rate recovery at 60s post-effort is a standard cardiorespiratory-fitness and autonomic marker that improves visibly with training. The torch requirement inverts the corpus's dim-gym problem into a feature — the lighting no longer matters because the phone supplies it. Between-round rest is compulsory and idle, so capture costs the athlete nothing. Face-based rPPG is the wrong choice here: sweat, motion, humidity and fluorescent flicker are exactly its failure modes, whereas contact PPG is robust to all four. (Flag: PPG-from-camera mechanism asserted from knowledge; validate on a mid-range Galaxy A before shipping any number.)

**Who fails it:** Nobody in either research pass does physiological sensing without a wearable. AIA requires a device. Samsung Health needs a Galaxy Watch for HRV. Naluri, BookDoc and every insurer app measure steps and questionnaires. The Malaysian market has zero autonomic data.

**Hybrid play:** 'Corner Cam': a 30-second finger-on-lens capture in the rest interval after each round. Derive HR at rest-start and at +60s → HRR60, and plot it as a new axis on the 6-axis stat radar — an axis that visibly improves over months and that no gym-mate on any other app can show. Optionally a morning readiness check feeding the Block Sim's fatigue model. Never present it as medical, never as a diagnosis, and show the confidence interval — the corpus is clear that overclaimed accuracy is the credibility failure mode of this whole category.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Velocity-based training is the established way to know when a set stopped being productive, and it normally costs hundreds to thousands of ringgit in hardware. A propped phone can approximate it — and autoregulation only ever needed RELATIVE velocity, which removes the calibration problem entirely.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. 4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. **Evidence:** Velocity-loss autoregulation (roughly 10-20% mean concentric velocity loss for strength/power intent, higher for hypertrophy) is a well-replicated strength-training finding, normally requiring a linear position transducer or accelerometer — GymAware, Vitruve, Enode. A 15% velocity drop is invisible to the naked eye, so this is a capability no coach possesses regardless of skill. Crucially, the threshold is defined relative to the FIRST rep of the same set, so absolute metric accuracy is irrelevant and 2D pose scale calibration is unnecessary. (Flag: VBT thresholds asserted from strength literature, not re-verified this pass.)

**Who fails it:** Berry Best's Gym Mode is a manual sets/weights/rest-timer logger with no camera. Fitness First's MyCoach is a progress log. Strong and Hevy — which the corpus names as what Malaysians actually use for lifting — are manual. Nobody in SEA does velocity.

**Hybrid play:** When the phone is propped for a barbell or dumbbell movement in Shadow Dojo, compute per-rep concentric duration and displacement → mean velocity, and render a live velocity-loss bar that ends the set at the prescribed threshold. Frame it in the existing tactical-telemetry language: the set ends when the telemetry says the reps stopped being worth doing. Be publicly honest about the limits — fixed camera required, noisy in 2D, better at relative loss and tempo consistency than absolute m/s — because honesty about limits is the exact credibility gap the photo-calorie category just fell into.

---

### [4/5 · large] Every product in this market is member-facing or owner-facing. Nothing is coach-facing. A head coach running a 20-person class can watch three people at a time and remembers the last thing they saw; one fixed phone can watch all twenty continuously.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

**Evidence:** Corpus on Vibefam: 'Owner-facing product: everything is optimised for the studio's revenue ops, not the athlete's experience.' On the whole category: 'Not one of them tracks what the athlete actually did.' And on monetisation, unambiguously: standalone consumer subscription revenue is not viable in Malaysia (AIA at RM10/mo, KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright, ClassPass RM99/RM249), so the realistic paths are bundling into membership or selling to the gym owner as retention software. Coach-facing is therefore both the empty quadrant and the monetisable one.

**Who fails it:** Vibefam, Mindbody, Glofox — all revenue ops. TRIBE's app is booking and packages. Celebrity Fitness ships check-in badges (attendance theatre). GymNation's genuine AI investment goes to sales calls and churn prediction, i.e. the business, never the coach.

**Hybrid play:** 'Mat Cam': an old Android on a tripod running the PWA in kiosk mode, multi-person pose, no video leaving the device. After class the coach receives six lines: 'Ali — left knee valgus on 11 of 14 round kicks. Siti — guard drops after round 2, third week running. Wei Ming — first session above his usual output, tell him.' That artefact is what a gym owner will actually pay for, because it makes the coach visibly better at the thing members are buying. It also generates the pairwise sparring data and the cohort dataset that the other gaps depend on, so it is the keystone build even though it is the largest.

### [4/5 · medium] One gym over three years is thousands of sessions across ~100 bodies with known outcomes — who got hurt, who progressed, who quit. That is a longitudinal cohort study. A head coach has anecdotes about it.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken.
