**Evidence:** GymNation is the only operator in either pass using AI on cohort data, and it uses it for churn prediction and AI sales calls — a business tool the member never sees. Everything else in Malaysia is voucher and step arithmetic. Meanwhile retention is the actual P&L problem for an independent Damansara gym, which the corpus identifies as the monetisation path, and the competitive pressure is ClassPass actively training members to deal-hop.

**Who fails it:** GymNation (operator-side only, and not in Malaysia). Vibefam (attendance counts, no outcomes). All insurer and government programmes (steps). Nobody surfaces cohort learning to the member.

**Hybrid play:** Two outputs from the same model. Member-facing, peer-referenced warnings, which persuade far better than generic rules: 'you are on a shin-conditioning volume ramp that 6 of 11 members with your profile did not tolerate.' Owner-facing, a weekly at-risk list WITH the reason: '3 members are in the week-3-to-5 attendance pattern that preceded 7 of the last 9 churns.' Hard guardrails: explicit consent surface, k-anonymity with no cohort surfaced below ~5 people, and never a name — a single gym is small enough that a careless cohort stat identifies an individual, which is both a PDPA exposure and a community-trust detonation.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Nobody instruments the non-decision. Every app in the market — including this one — begins recording at the moment of the workout, so the modal user event (deciding NOT to go) is invisible, unlogged and has zero product surface. The churn happens in a 90-minute window the software never sees.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** Milkman et al., 'Megastudies improve the impact of applied behavioural science' (Nature, 2021) ran 53 interventions across 61,293 members of 24 Hour Fitness over 28 days. The top performer was not a streak, a badge or a leaderboard — it was planning prompts plus a micro-incentive for RETURNING AFTER A MISSED WORKOUT (~27% lift in weekly visits). The winning lever acted on the miss, not the session. Gollwitzer & Sheeran's meta-analysis of implementation intentions (94 studies, Advances in Experimental Social Psychology 2006) puts the effect of if-then plans specifying WHEN, WHERE and HOW at roughly d=0.65 — the largest cheap lever in the whole behaviour-change toolkit, and it must be set BEFORE the moment of temptation. Against this, the brief's own baseline: ~75% of health app users quit within two weeks, i.e. before enough sessions exist for any XP curve to matter.

**Who fails it:** ClassPass Malaysia (exists only at the booking transaction; a non-booking is a silent revenue loss). Vibefam and TRIBE (booking front-ends — the member relationship between sessions is explicitly unclaimed, which the researchers flag as 'the clearest whitespace'). Celebrity Fitness (badges fire on check-in, so a non-check-in produces nothing). AIA Vitality (structurally worse — it awards points ONLY for auto-captured workout data, so a miss is not just unrewarded, it is unrepresented). Strava (entirely post-hoc). Fitness First MyCoach (a log).

**Hybrid play:** THE SLOT — a pre-commitment surface that runs before the week starts. Every Sunday night the athlete taps three concrete windows against the gym's real timetable (day + time + named coach/class), which is a literal implementation intention, not a goal. At T-90min the HUD pushes exactly ONE tactical line with the friction pre-solved from data it already has ('Bag work 20:00. Grab 14 min from your usual pin. 40 XP to Level 9. Coach Amir is on.'). If unconfirmed at T-30, it does NOT nag — it offers a graded downgrade so the outcome is never binary: FULL / SHADOW DOJO 20min at home / MOBILITY 8min. And the piece nobody has: if they decline, one tap logs WHY from five buttons (kerja, penat, sakit, jam, family). Within a month the gym owns the only decline-reason dataset in Malaysian fitness, and the HUD can start pre-empting the specific excuse — 'Last three Tuesdays you bailed on traffic. There's a 21:15 slot.'

---

### [4/5 · medium] The comeback is unrewarded everywhere. Every app treats a returning lapsed user as a degraded version of their old self — it shows them the loss, a stale programme and a dead streak — when the return is the single highest-leverage behavioural moment the product will ever get.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** The Nature 2021 megastudy's winning intervention was literally a bonus for returning after a missed workout. Dai, Milkman & Riis, 'The Fresh Start Effect' (Management Science, 2014): temporal landmarks — new week, new month, birthday, post-holiday — produce sharp, measurable spikes in goal pursuit because they let people relegate past failure to a former self. Charness & Gneezy, 'Incentives to Exercise' (Econometrica, 2009) found paid attendance produced durable habit specifically among the previously-inactive, because the payment bought enough REPETITIONS for habit to form — i.e. the job of a reward is to buy the first few sessions back, exactly the comeback window. Lally et al. (European Journal of Social Psychology, 2010) found median ~66 days to automaticity and, crucially, that a single missed occasion did NOT materially impair habit formation — the lapse is only fatal if the system tells the user it is.

**Who fails it:** All of them, without exception. ClassPass expires credits during exactly the holiday when everyone lapses. AIA Vitality simply stops paying (and pays nothing for the return). TRIBE's expiring class pack converts a lapse into a financial loss. Celebrity Fitness / Fitness First send generic win-back marketing. Strava shows you your fitness decay curve, which is the most demoralising possible artefact.

**Hybrid play:** THE REACTIVATION CONTRACT. If the gap is >= 10 days, do not render the HUD at all — the HUD is a scoreboard and the returner is losing on it. Render one screen instead: a 6-second clip of the athlete's OWN best round from before the lapse, pulled from the Shadow Dojo (their own body, their own best moment — this is the asset no competitor has), and one button: 'RESTART AT 60%'. Accepting generates a deliberately easy 3-session re-entry ladder, with the comeback session carrying the LARGEST single XP award in the entire economy — coming back must be worth more than never leaving — and it fires one WhatsApp line to a nominated training partner: 'Fikri is back Thursday 20:00.' Then anchor the restart to the nearest fresh-start landmark (Monday, 1st of the month, first day after Raya) rather than 'today', because the literature says the landmark itself does work.

---

### [4/5 · medium] XP and levels reward VOLUME, so they stop working the instant volume plateaus — and nothing in the entire Malaysian market rewards getting better at the same thing. This is the deepest strategic gap available, because it is the one that requires pose estimation, which is empty whitespace locally.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** The researchers' own headline finding: every behavioural incentive in Malaysia — AIA Vitality (up to 150 physical-activity points/day), Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern — is step-count or duration based, so 'a 90-minute Muay Thai session, a heavy squat day, or 200 rounds on the bag produce almost no steps and therefore score near zero everywhere.' Combat and strength athletes are systematically invisible to the whole incentive layer. And of the studio software these gyms actually run on (Vibefam, Mindbody, Glofox): 'Not one of them tracks what the athlete actually did' — belt rank is metadata recording what a coach declared. Behaviourally, a volume-XP curve also fails on its own terms: Kivetz/Urminsky/Zheng's goal-gradient work shows motivation collapses after each milestone, and a standard stretching XP curve widens the gap between milestones exactly as novelty decays — the reward schedule quietly slides from continuous to extinction, which is the classic setup for abandonment.

**Who fails it:** AIA Vitality (points for auto-captured duration/steps only). Activ@Work and KOSPEN@Activ (steps). Celebrity Fitness (badges for check-ins — 'attendance theatre'). ClassPass (credits per class attended). Strava (GPS distance, structurally useless indoors). Fittr and cult.fit (no vision, no movement scoring). Hybrid Combative HUD's own XP curve, to the extent it pays per session rather than per improvement.

**Hybrid play:** Make the Shadow Dojo the XP ENGINE and pay for DELTA, not dose. Ship a TECHNIQUE LEDGER: each named technique (jab-cross, teep, level change, hip escape) carries its own mastery track scored against the athlete's own history, not a global standard. Award the big XP for: a personal-best form score on a named technique; holding 90% form at a higher cadence than last month; and — the most under-used and most honest skill proxy — VARIANCE REDUCTION, i.e. rep 40 of a round looking like rep 5. Consistency-under-fatigue is what a coach actually means by 'getting better', it is directly measurable from MediaPipe joint angles on-device, and it keeps paying out long after volume has flattened. Frame it in-product against the market: 'AIA gives you zero points for tonight. We gave you 340, and here's the frame where your guard dropped.'

---

### [4/5 · small] Weekly bounties are assigned BY the app, which is the exact structure that erodes intrinsic motivation. Nobody in this market lets the athlete author their own goal, and nobody attaches a real commitment device to it.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** Deci, Koestner & Ryan's meta-analysis (Psychological Bulletin, 1999; 128 experiments) found that expected tangible rewards contingent on task engagement reliably UNDERMINE intrinsic motivation, while autonomy-supportive framing does not — imposed goals with imposed rewards are the worst configuration in the literature. Against that, commitment devices with self-set stakes show durable effects: Royer, Stehr & Sydnor (AEJ: Applied Economics, 2015) found a self-funded commitment contract sustained gym attendance well after the incentive period, whereas the Nature 2021 megastudy's imposed-incentive gains largely decayed within about ten weeks of the intervention ending. Self-authorship plus a witness plus a small social cost is the highest-evidence, lowest-cost combination available.

**Who fails it:** AIA Vitality (its 'personalised Weekly Challenge' adapts to you but is still handed to you, and is redeemed for cash — maximal extrinsic framing). Activ@Work (imposed, and the group-photo bonus is 'trivially gameable'). WeWard (payout so low that 'users do the maths' and disengage — a live demonstration of a reward that destroys motivation). Hybrid Combative HUD's current weekly bounties, insofar as the app writes them.

**Hybrid play:** Convert bounties into DECLARATIONS. The athlete writes their own line on Sunday ('4 sessions, one of them sparring, no bailing on Tuesday') and stakes something real but non-financial: (a) a WITNESS — one named training partner receives a WhatsApp-pasteable card on Sunday night showing whether it landed, or (b) a FORFEIT drawn from a gym-authored list (mat cleanup, buy the coach teh tarik, 100 burpees at open mat). Keep app-generated bounties only as a fallback for people who don't declare, and mark them visibly as the lesser option. Two further details that matter: let the athlete set the difficulty themselves (autonomy is the active ingredient), and show the historical hit-rate of their own declarations so the goal-setting itself becomes a skill they can level.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Social accountability is aimed at strangers. /brag targets Instagram — an audience that does not know whether you showed up — while the three people who train next to you, and the WhatsApp group that is the actual operating system of Malaysian gym life, get nothing.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** The brief's own baseline: social accountability roughly doubles adherence. The Köhler motivation-gain effect (Feltz, Irwin and colleagues, exercise-psychology work on virtually-present partners) shows persistence on exercise tasks rising substantially — up to roughly double in lab conditions — when a person works with a moderately superior partner under CONJUNCTIVE scoring, where the team's result is determined by the weaker member. Kullgren et al. (Annals of Internal Medicine, 2013) found group-based financial incentives outperformed individual incentives for weight loss. And the researchers' single most actionable Malaysian observation: 'WhatsApp is the operating system of Malaysian life, including gym life... Any product that fights WhatsApp loses; a product that produces something worth pasting INTO a WhatsApp group wins (this is a stronger local play than the Instagram story card).'

**Who fails it:** /brag as currently framed (Instagram story cards reach the younger boutique demographic only, and are broadcast, not accountability). Celebrity Fitness 'squad challenges' (check-in based). Activ@Work team photos (gameable). Strava clubs (running/cycling, GPS-only, useless indoors). ClassPass (actively anti-community — it trains promiscuous sampling; the researchers call it 'hostile to a gym building a committed combat-sports community'). Vibefam (no member-to-member surface at all).

**Hybrid play:** FIRE TEAMS — coach-assigned 3-person cells with CONJUNCTIVE weekly scoring: the cell's score is the MINIMUM of its three members, not the sum. This is the single highest-leverage line of code in this whole document, because it makes the strongest member's own progression depend on dragging the weakest one in, which converts your best athletes into an unpaid retention team. Attach two outputs: (1) a WhatsApp-native square PNG with a pre-written Manglish caption, generated from the same canvas code /brag already uses — near-zero build, and it reaches the aunties and uncles Instagram never will; (2) a PING button that fires one line to your fire team ('Aku pergi 20:00, sapa nak join?'). The gym is already a community that physically exists — the app's job is to be its nervous system between sessions, not a broadcast channel to people who aren't in it.

---

### [4/5 · small] Brag cards point outward at an audience; the artefacts that actually protect against churn point inward at the athlete's future self and sideways at the coach. The product currently ships the vanity output and neither of the durable ones.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** Bryan et al. (PNAS, 2011) found noun-framing identity ('be a voter') produced significantly higher actual turnout than verb-framing the behaviour ('to vote') — identity, not activity, is what survives a gap in behaviour. Market corroboration on the weakness of share cards: Celebrity Fitness ships shareable achievement badges and virtual squad challenges and it has not stopped chain-gym churn, because 'badges are check-in based — attendance theatre.' Conversely, JomRun's real retention came from a PHYSICAL medal and jersey mailed to the runner, not from a share graphic — a tangible identity token beat a social post. And the researchers' Malaysian channel finding again: the shareable that matters locally is the one you paste into WhatsApp, not Instagram.

**Who fails it:** /brag in its current framing. Celebrity Fitness badges. Strava's share culture (which set the expectation but is GPS-only). AIA Vitality (rewards are cash and vouchers — pure transaction, zero identity). WeWard (collectible WeCards, but tied to walking to locations, and the payout maths destroys the illusion).

**Hybrid play:** Two artefacts, both small builds on canvas code that already exists. (1) THE DOSSIER — a monthly auto-generated personnel file, deliberately not a highlight reel, WhatsApp-pasteable: 'OPERATOR FIKRI · 14 months · 212 rounds · jab-cross mastery 78% · longest lapse survived: 23 days, returned 4 Mac.' Including the survived lapse is the key move — it makes 'someone who comes back' a documented part of the identity, which is the specific belief that protects against the NEXT lapse. (2) THE TIME CAPSULE — at signup and every 90 days, a private 10-second selfie video answering 'why are you here?', stored on-device, and replayed automatically at exactly one moment: the reactivation screen after a 10+ day gap. Your own face from three months ago beats any notification copy a solo dev can write, costs nothing to generate, and is impossible for a chain gym to make feel sincere.

---

### [4/5 · medium] The seasonal collapse is fully predictable and nobody pre-loads for it. Ramadan, Hari Raya, CNY, Deepavali and school holidays are scheduled churn events, and the entire market either ignores them or actively penalises them.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** The researchers call Ramadan 'the most obviously under-served seasonal moment in the entire market' and document exactly what changes: training windows shift to roughly an hour before iftar or after iftar and terawih (about 21:30-midnight); volume drops to ~3-4 sessions/week at 30-45 minutes; hydration compresses into the iftar-to-sahur window; and Hari Raya adds 'a week-plus of travel back to kampung and heavy eating'. Only two products in the whole pack show any Ramadan awareness — MalaysianCalorie (sahur/iftar times, bazaar guide) and JomRun ('Fit Ramadhan' virtual run) — and NEITHER touches training. Layer on Dai/Milkman/Riis's fresh-start effect: the day after a landmark is the strongest re-entry moment in the calendar, which means Raya is simultaneously the biggest churn risk and the biggest reactivation opportunity of the Malaysian year.

**Who fails it:** Every training product. AIA Vitality and Activ@Work keep counting steps through a month when daytime activity is deliberately reduced. ClassPass credits expire during Raya travel. TRIBE's 1-month class-pack expiry lands badly across any holiday. Fitness First / Anytime Fitness contracts run regardless. Vibefam schedules classes with no prayer-time or fasting awareness — the researchers flag 'zero localisation for BM, halal, Ramadan scheduling or prayer-time-aware class slots' as the incumbents' shared blind spot.

**Hybrid play:** A calendar-aware CAMPAIGN system, pre-loaded once and reused every year. Two weeks before Ramadan the HUD announces PUASA PROTOCOL: targets re-base to 3x30-45min, recommended windows move to pre-iftar or post-terawih, shields auto-arm, hydration guidance in /fuel switches to the iftar-to-sahur window (which is also a better and more honest feature than a generic 16:8 timer). BALIK KAMPUNG MODE: a 12-minute Shadow Dojo circuit needing 2m x 2m and no equipment, fully offline, with the fire team still scoring — so the person in Kelantan for ten days still has a live relationship with the gym. Then the fresh-start hook: a RAYA RESET cohort that starts the day after and re-baselines EVERYONE together on the same day. Cohort restarts beat individual restarts because the awkwardness of being detrained is socialised rather than personal — and no competitor in Malaysia is even trying.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Small-community scoreboards break in ways global apps never have to solve: a 150-person gym leaderboard demotivates the bottom 80%, and any score derived from self-report gets gamed in a room where everyone knows each other, which destroys the board's meaning.

**Lens:** RETENTION AND BEHAVIOUR — the gap between apps that COLLECT data and apps that CHANGE what a person does at 6:15pm on a Tuesday when they are tired and it is raining in Damansara. Analysed against the five researchers' market pack plus the behavioural-science literature on habit formation, commitment devices, loss aversion, self-determination theory and gamification failure modes. METHOD NOTE: this session's live web-search budget was already exhausted by earlier researchers, so no new searches could be run. Every market claim is traced to the supplied research pack; every behavioural claim is traced to named, published literature I can cite precisely (author, year, journal/finding) — these are literature citations from knowledge, not freshly re-fetched pages, and the solo dev should spot-check the three that carry the most design weight (Sharif & Shu 2017 on emergency reserves, Milkman et al. 2021 megastudy, Deci/Koestner/Ryan 1999) before quoting them publicly. CORE THESIS: Hybrid Combative HUD currently has a rich TELEMETRY layer and a thin BEHAVIOUR layer. XP, levels, the 6-axis radar, streaks and brag cards are all outputs — they describe a session that already happened. Every one of the six failure moments the brief names (the decision not to train, the streak break, the two-week return, the plateau, the injury layoff, the extrinsic-to-intrinsic handoff) happens when the app is CLOSED, and the app currently has no product surface for any of them. Meanwhile the single biggest structural finding in the market pack — that Malaysia's entire incentive layer (AIA Vitality, Activ@Work, KOSPEN, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-count based and therefore scores a 90-minute Muay Thai session at approximately zero — means combat athletes are behaviourally unserved by design, not by accident. That is the opening. The second opening is that in a SINGLE gym, the highest-retention asset is not software at all: it is a coach who says your name and references your left hook from two Tuesdays ago. No competitor in this pack — not Vibefam, not ClassPass, not Naluri — puts training evidence into that coach's hands.

**Evidence:** The Köhler motivation-gain effect only operates in a narrow band — persistence rises when the partner is MODERATELY superior (the effect is strongest around a modest performance gap and disappears or reverses when the gap is large), which is precisely why a whole-gym ranking motivates the top few and demoralises everyone else. On gameability, the market gives two live examples the researchers flag directly: Activ@Work's monthly group-activity-photo bonus is 'trivially gameable', and JomRun's virtual-run proof submission is 'manually verifiable and gameable'. Celebrity Fitness badges reward check-ins, which in a small gym everyone can see is not the same as training. In a single-gym product the scoreboard's credibility is a social fact, not a technical one — if members believe it can be faked, it stops functioning as social proof at all.

**Who fails it:** Celebrity Fitness (check-in badges, club and squad challenges). Activ@Work (photo bonus). JomRun (submitted proof). WeWard (step-based, spoofable). Strava (segment leaderboards are famously gamed, and are GPS-only anyway). AIA Vitality (partly mitigated by requiring auto-captured data — which is also exactly why it locks out a camera-only combat app, per the market pack).

**Hybrid play:** Never render a global rank. Show exactly ONE other person: the RIVAL — auto-matched each week to a member inside a deliberately narrow performance band above you (target roughly 1.1-1.4x, the band where the Köhler effect actually operates), shown under a callsign unless both sides opt in to identify. Frame it as a gap, not a rank: 'You are 3 rounds behind SHRIKE this week.' Re-roll the rival weekly so nobody is permanently beaten. Then protect the board's credibility with evidence-based scoring: XP derives from pose data or console OCR with a confidence score attached, never from a self-reported checkbox, and any manually corrected entry carries a visible marker. In a gym where everyone trains in the same room, a scoreboard people trust is worth more than a scoreboard that is easy to fill.

---

### [4/5 · small] Macro engines assume three daytime meals. During Ramadan the whole day's intake compresses into iftar → post-terawih → sahur, and the highest-calorie event of the month — the buka puasa bazaar — is treated as an edge case by every tracker.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** Only MalaysianCalorie ships sahur/iftar times and a Ramadan bazaar guide. Berry Best ships generic 16:8 and OMAD fasting timers with no sahur/iftar anchor. MyNutriDiari (the government app with authoritative MOH food data) has no meal-timing model at all. One single-source, unverified report suggests MyFitnessPal has added Suhoor/Iftar logging — worth verifying because it is the only competitive movement in this space. Bazaar staples (murtabak, kuih lapis, ayam percik, popia, air bandung) are dense, gravy-heavy and portioned by the vendor, not the eater.

**Who fails it:** Berry Best (generic IF windows), MyNutriDiari (no timing model), MyFitnessPal / FatSecret / Cal AI / Samsung Health (Western meal-pattern assumptions baked into the daily target logic), and every chain gym app (no nutrition layer at all).

**Hybrid play:** Replace the generic fasting timer in /fuel with a three-block plan sharing the same prayer calculation as the Puasa Protocol: IFTAR (break and rehydrate, protein floor), POST-TERAWIH (the real training meal, carbs to refill), SAHUR (slow carbs plus the day's fluid load). Each block gets its own macro and fluid target rather than one daily number the user has no way to distribute. Then add a BAZAAR capture mode: one photo of the whole bag or tray, with the Claude vision call run against an injected Ramadan-bazaar dish prior (~120 curated items) plus per-item confidence and a tap-to-remove list. The cost is a prompt change and a curated list, not a new model — and it is the one month of the year when a nutrition feature is genuinely load-bearing.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Every photo-macro app assumes one plate equals one person's meal, made of separable items. Malaysian eating is point-at-dishes nasi campur, family-style shared plates, and gravy poured over everything — the exact worst case, and nobody models the sharing or the gravy.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** The NIH/ASN 2026 study (Hengist et al., 102 nutrient-controlled meals, presented at NUTRITION 2026) measured Cal AI at -345 kcal, Lose It! -333, MyFitnessPal -327 and Appediet -252 — all roughly 33% underestimates, on WESTERN PLATED meals. HealthifyMe's own reported Snap accuracy is 60-70% (about 75% with a human-hybrid technique) on its home Indian cuisine. Samsung's on-device model benchmarks at a 64.1% food ID rate with ±26% portion error (MAPE). FatSecret returns nasi lemak at 145 kcal/100g, 261 kcal/serving and 348 kcal/cup for the same dish. MyDietPRO explicitly names 'nasi campur lunches' as the problem and still solves only the single-eater case.

**Who fails it:** Cal AI, MyFitnessPal Photo, Samsung Health, HealthifyMe Snap, Berry Best, MalaysianCalorie, BookDoc's food log — all single-plate, single-eater, no gravy model, no portion modifiers a Malaysian would recognise.

**Hybrid play:** Two flows the incumbents structurally lack. (a) KONGSI MODE: photograph the table once, tap how many people are eating, then drag a per-dish share slider ('most of the sotong, none of the rendang'). The LLM returns per-dish totals and the UI does the division — so the user corrects a SHARE, which they know exactly, instead of a calorie count, which they don't. (b) NASI CAMPUR DECOMPOSE: return an itemised lauk list with per-item confidence and tap-to-remove, plus two one-tap modifiers worth more than any model improvement — 'banjir kuah' (gravy-flooded, adds santan/oil calories that vision cannot see under the sauce) and rice portion (separuh / biasa / lebih). Make mamak drinks a first-class enum rather than free text: teh tarik / kopi O / limau ais crossed with kurang manis / kosong / siew dai. That is a ±100 kcal swing the user knows precisely and no app has ever asked for.

---

### [4/5 · small] Heat and humidity are the hidden training load in Malaysia and nothing measures them. A session at 33°C and 84% RH is not the same session as one in San Francisco, but every app scores output as if the environment were neutral — so Malaysian effort is systematically undervalued and hydration guidance is generic.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** The research documents ~80%+ humidity year-round, monsoon season and periodic transboundary haze pushing training indoors and making outdoor/GPS apps 'structurally weak for a big part of the year'. It also documents the gym-floor consequences: humidity means sweaty hands and screens that mis-register touch, basements have poor mobile signal so offline-first with queued sync is 'not optional', and dim flickering light degrades both OCR and pose detection. Not one app in the entire survey ingests temperature, humidity or air quality as a training variable.

**Who fails it:** Strava, WeWard, AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, Samsung Health and Apple Fitness+ are all environment-blind — and the step-based schemes double-penalise, because they are outdoor-biased in a climate that forces training indoors for months at a time.

**Hybrid play:** A HEAT LEDGER on /hud. (a) Ingest temperature and relative humidity for Damansara from a free weather API (one cached call per session) and apply a heat-strain multiplier to session XP: '48 MIN @ 33°C / 84% RH → HEAT-ADJUSTED OUTPUT +19%'. This is arithmetic, not AI, and it is the first metric that makes a Malaysian athlete's actual difficulty legible. (b) Sweat rate with no wearable: prompt a weigh-in before and after one session a week, compute litres/hour, convert to a personal mL prescription — which during Ramadan is automatically re-expressed as a fluid load to be consumed between iftar and sahur, because it cannot be consumed during training. (c) Haze season: when the DOE Air Pollutant Index crosses 100, the weekly bounty board auto-swaps any outdoor bounty for a Shadow Dojo equivalent and states why. All three are cheap; all three are unmistakably Malaysian.

---

### [4/5 · small] Apps localise by translating the interface, or not at all — while still demanding structured English INPUT through pickers and search boxes. Malaysians code-switch when they type, and on a humid gym floor they cannot type at all.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** The research states plainly that real usage is code-switched — 'nasi lemak biasa tapi tak nak sambal', '2 roti canai + teh tarik kurang manis' — and that the practical guidance is 'don't translate the UI, tolerate mixed-language INPUT'. MyKalori's entire thesis is exactly this (message it in Manglish, get calories in ~3 seconds, no app download) and it is still in private beta. Naluri, with ~US$19.9M raised, offers English, Indonesian and Thai but NO Bahasa Melayu website. MyDietPRO, Joompa and every chain gym app default to English. And separately: the research found NO health or fitness app in Malaysia with any Tamil support at all — a genuine equity gap in the country whose mamak food culture is Tamil-Muslim.

**Who fails it:** Naluri, Joompa, MyDietPRO, Fitness First / Celebrity Fitness / Anytime Fitness apps, MyFitnessPal, Strava — none accept mixed-language free text. MyNutriDiari is BM-first but manual-search only, which is the friction that kills adherence.

**Hybrid play:** One Manglish command bar spanning /fuel, /hud and /scanner, backed by a single LLM parse-to-JSON call. '3 round sparring dgn Adi lepas tu 20 min bag' becomes a session record; 'makan nasi campur ayam masak merah + sayur, kuah banjir' becomes a meal. Never translate the HUD chrome — the tactical English IS the brand — just never reject the input. Two multipliers: (i) voice input matters more here than anywhere, because sweaty hands mis-register touch and mid-set typing is impossible, so the same parser behind speech-to-text is the humid-gym interface; (ii) accept Tamil and Tamil-transliterated input ('iru thosai'), which is a prompt change costing effectively nothing and would make this the first fitness app in Malaysia to do it.

---

### [4/5 · small] Prayer times and the Malaysian festival calendar structure the training year, and no scheduler or streak system knows either exists.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** Beyond Ramadan the research identifies predictable adherence collapses at Hari Raya ('a week-plus of travel back to kampung and heavy eating'), Chinese New Year, Deepavali and the school-holiday calendar, and calls out that global software has no concept of prayer-time-aware class slots — noting Mindbody and Glofox have 'zero localisation for BM, halal, Ramadan scheduling or prayer-time-aware class slots'. On the gym floor the ordinary week also has hard blackouts: the Maghrib gap most evenings and Jumaah roughly 12:30-14:30 on Fridays.

**Who fails it:** Every gamification product in the market — Celebrity Fitness check-in badges, WeWard, AIA Vitality's weekly challenge, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ — plus Duolingo-style daily streaks in global apps, all of which read a religious or family obligation as user failure.

**Hybrid play:** Ship the Malaysian calendar as CONTENT, not merely as leniency. (a) The session planner is prayer-aware from the same on-device calculation: it never proposes a block straddling Maghrib, and it knows the floor empties for ~25 minutes then refills — which is also a real scheduling insight for the coach. (b) BALIK KAMPUNG PROTOCOL: the week before Raya, the HUD pre-emptively converts the collapse into a scripted 7-day quest — four 12-minute bodyweight sessions scored by the EXISTING Shadow Dojo pose engine in your aunt's living room (no equipment, no gym), a rendang/lemang/kuih portion card in /fuel, and Raya shields that hold the streak intact. Same shape for CNY and Deepavali. This converts the year's biggest churn window into the year's best content moment, and reuses code that is already built.
