### [4/5 · small] Console OCR has to work on a mixed fleet of LED-segment displays, in dim flickering light, through a humidity-fogged lens, on a mid-range Android with no signal. A global app must solve this generally and therefore cannot. A single-gym app does not have to.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** The research warns to 'expect to need per-console calibration' across the mixed Malaysian fleet of Life Fitness, Technogym, Matrix, Precor and cheaper local imports, notes that LED-segment displays 'behave badly under camera rolling shutter', and that mixed, dim, fluorescent-flickering light degrades both OCR and pose detection. Its prescription is a torch prompt, a framing guide, confidence scores and a manual-correct path 'rather than silently guessing'. It also states that in gym basements 'offline-first with queued sync is not optional'.

**Who fails it:** No competitor attempts console OCR at all — but the meaningful failure is structural: no global product could ever maintain per-machine display templates for one gym in Damansara, and no studio SaaS (Vibefam, Mindbody) captures what happened on the equipment at all.

**Hybrid play:** Turn the constraint into a proprietary data asset. Stick a short code on each machine frame (TM-03, XT-01, BIKE-05). On the first scan of that machine, the member corrects the fields once; the corrected field geometry, display type and colour profile are saved as a GYM-WIDE template keyed to that code, so every subsequent scan by any member snaps to it — faster, more accurate, less correction. Add forced torch, a 'LAP CERMIN' (wipe your lens) prompt when frame contrast drops below threshold, an offline queue that syncs on exit, and confidence-gated manual correction. Within a month the gym's entire fleet is calibrated by its own members: a dataset that exists nowhere else, cannot be bought, and gets better precisely because the product serves one gym.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Every plausible consumer price for this app is above what the market will bear, the payment rail most apps ship (USD card) silently excludes a large share of Malaysians, and the one buyer with budget — the gym owner — has no product built for him beyond booking ops.

**Lens:** MALAYSIAN LOCAL CONTEXT — gaps that exist because global fitness apps encode Western assumptions about the body, the day, the plate, the wallet and the weather.

The core thesis: every app in this market is localised at the wrong layer. They translate interfaces and add local dishes to a database, while leaving four Western defaults untouched — (1) that a day has three daytime meals and no fasting month, (2) that a plate belongs to one person and its contents are separable, (3) that a body is at risk above BMI 25, and (4) that the environment is neutral, the wallet holds a credit card, and the athlete wears a watch. Meanwhile the entire Malaysian incentive layer (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, insurer schemes) pays on step counts and auto-captured device data, which makes combat and strength athletes structurally invisible — 90 minutes of Muay Thai scores near zero everywhere in the country.

Three structural facts shape which gaps are worth closing. First, no Malaysian app does computer vision on MOVEMENT — every verifiable local "AI" claim is food-photo recognition or chatbot triage — so the Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset and photo-to-macros is the commodity. Second, Malaysians will not pay standalone consumer subscription prices for a wellness layer (AIA Vitality anchors at RM10/month AND pays cashback; KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright), so the gym owner and the tax office are the realistic payers, not the member. Third, the single-gym constraint is an asset, not a limitation: per-machine OCR calibration, a WhatsApp group leaderboard and a coach retention console are all things a global product cannot maintain for one gym in Damansara.

The highest-leverage unclaimed territory is Ramadan as a TRAINING protocol (not a nutrition mode), halal moved off the food photo and onto the supplement tub where it is answerable, Asian metabolic-risk cutoffs with a camera-only vital sign, and the Malaysian calendar treated as content rather than as streak failure. Almost all of it is prompt engineering, arithmetic and canvas rendering — solo-dev-shaped work with a camera and an LLM.

**Evidence:** AIA Vitality is RM10/month AND is net cash-positive to the user (RM10 weekly cashback, RM100-300 gym vouchers, 20% off Fitness First and Celebrity Fitness) — a brutal reference price. KFit's RM99/month unlimited model failed outright and the company became Fave's voucher business. MyFitnessPal's and Strava's USD billing is actively resented, and many Malaysians have no international card. Roughly 85-90% of adults use an e-wallet; Touch 'n Go has 23M+ verified users and gained RECURRING billing in March 2026; FPX is the workhorse above ~RM50 and DuitNow QR is one code accepting every bank and wallet. Vibefam's explicit competitive attack on Mindbody and Glofox is precisely their weak local payment-rail support — that is the local fault line. And an annually-renewing LHDN sports/gym tax relief exists that no fitness app exploits (this research cites RM500; the sports-specific relief was raised in a recent budget, so VERIFY the current YA figure before it goes in any UI).

**Who fails it:** MyFitnessPal, Strava, Cal AI and HealthifyMe (USD or foreign billing, no local rails); Mindbody and Glofox (card-centric, USD/EUR-priced against RM revenue); Vibefam (correct rails but purely owner-facing ops, no member-facing product); and every app in the market on the tax relief, which none of them touch.

**Hybrid play:** Three moves. (a) Never charge the member — the HUD is bundled into the Hybrid membership, and the paying customer is the gym owner, who gets a COACH CONSOLE showing exactly who is drifting (days since last session, broken streak, declining form score, missed bounties) with a one-tap Manglish WhatsApp nudge. That is retention software Vibefam does not sell and cannot, because it never sees what the athlete did. (b) Any direct charge settles via DuitNow QR / FPX / TnG recurring, priced in RM, never a card form. (c) TAX SHIELD: an LHDN receipt vault — photograph gym receipts, class packs and gear; Claude vision OCRs vendor, amount and date and bins them against the qualifying relief categories; a gauge on the HUD reads 'RM 340 / RM 500 CLAIMED' and a one-tap year-end PDF bundle drops every January. Real money returned to the member, a December re-up incentive the owner will love, and the research found not one app doing it.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Every 'AI wellness' vital-sign product scans the FACE. Contact fingertip PPG (finger on rear lens + torch) is dramatically more robust and is the only method with published evidence of skin-tone neutrality — and nobody in Malaysia ships either.

**Lens:** HARDWARE-FREE SENSING — how close is WHOOP-class readiness with zero hardware?

BOTTOM LINE: A phone gets you roughly 70% of WHOOP's readiness stack, and 0% of the 30% that constitutes WHOOP's actual moat. Chasing the remaining 30% is the trap. Inverting the axis is the win.

WHAT IS GENUINELY WHOOP-CLASS TODAY:
• Heart rate via CONTACT fingertip PPG (finger over rear lens + torch on): 1.6% ± 4.3% MAPE vs reference in 95 subjects (Google Health, Communications Medicine 2022). Critically, there was NO skin-tone gradient — 1.8% (very light–intermediate), 1.3% (tan/brown), 1.8% (dark) — because the torch transilluminates the finger rather than reflecting off melanin. For a Malaysian gym this is decisive.
• Respiratory rate from front camera, subject sitting still: MAE 0.78 ± 0.61 breaths/min (n=50), holding at 0.80 ± 0.60 in people with chronic respiratory disease. WHOOP charges money for this number.
• Resting HR trend: trivially reachable, and one of the more robust illness/overreaching signals in existence.

WHAT IS NOT:
• HRV. A camera-PPG app produced 17.49% MAPE on RMSSD against 2.16% for a Polar chest strap in 37 trained athletes (Johansson et al., Frontiers in Physiology 2025). Day-to-day biological variation in lnRMSSD is only ~3.1–5.8% (Flatt 2019). So a SINGLE camera HRV reading is mostly measurement noise — it becomes signal only as a 7-day rolling mean (√7 ≈ 2.6× noise reduction). Frequency-domain HRV (LF/HF) is unusable: it destabilises at 10% data degradation while median IBI survives to 60%.
• FACIAL rPPG in a gym. Three independent failure modes stack exactly where Hybrid operates: melanin (respiratory error 0.45 bpm light-skinned vs 0.80 dark-skinned; Fitzpatrick VI carried OR 5.08 for measurement failure in a BP study), elevated heart rate (5 of 8 rPPG methods degraded significantly at high HR — and notably low illumination mattered LESS than high HR, per the CHILL dataset, npj Digital Medicine 2025), and motion/sweat. The pretty demo (point front camera at face between rounds) is the single worst engineering choice available. Use the finger and the torch.
• Sleep staging and continuous in-session HR. Structurally unreachable — both require a device on the body while the user is not holding one. Do not fake these.

THE INVERSION — the signature that runs through the entire pose literature:
Cameras measure TIME, FREQUENCY and VELOCITY well. They measure MAGNITUDE, ABSOLUTE POSITION and DEPTH badly. Evidence is remarkably consistent: tremor peak frequency was accurate while tremor amplitude was "insufficient for clinical application"; markerless boxing showed elbow position errors >3 cm while segment velocities showed "good-to-excellent agreement"; MediaPipe knee flexion MAE 3.15–4.10° but ankle "poor concordance"; even two-camera MediaPipe 3D RMSE ranged 7.88 mm (knee medial-lateral) to 54.55 mm (ankle anterior-posterior); gait symmetry was the single weakest derived metric ("moderate to weak"). Single-camera z-depth is not usable, full stop.

That signature points at exactly one readiness marker that is combat-specific AND better on a phone than on any wrist strap: PUNCH VELOCITY DECAY. Fatigue significantly reduced punch velocity and impact force in MMA fighters (p<0.01), and 2D video agreed with 3D infrared motion capture at r = 0.964–0.999, ICC 0.81–0.99. WHOOP cannot see this. Neither can any Malaysian competitor — no Malaysian app does movement CV at all.

THE BIGGEST WIN IS NOT A SENSOR:
Lab-condition MediaPipe rep counting hits 97.2% accuracy (MAE 0.06 reps). The same task with real users placing their own phones hits 61.1% for push-ups and 61.5% for squats (JMIR mHealth 2026, n=44). Within that same study, correct diagonal framing at 1.8–2.0 m recovered 85.7–95.5% (MAE 0.05–0.28), while side-on at 90 cm gave 0% detection and front-on at 360 cm gave 20%. A 36-point accuracy gap is sitting in camera-placement UX, not in the model. This is the highest-ROI engineering work available to a solo dev, and it costs a reticle and a distance check.

VERDICT ON "WHOOP-CLASS READINESS WITH ZERO HARDWARE": Reachable for heart rate, respiratory rate and resting-HR trend. Reachable-with-caveats for HRV if and only if you ship 7-day rolling means and refuse to display daily numbers. Unreachable for sleep and continuous strain. But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** Google Health prospective validation (Communications Medicine 2022, n=95): finger-over-rear-camera HR gave MAPE 1.6% ± 4.3%, with essentially no skin-tone gradient — 1.8% ± 4.5% (very light to intermediate), 1.3% ± 3.3% (tan and brown), 1.8% ± 4.9% (dark). The torch transilluminates the finger rather than reflecting off melanin. Facial rPPG shows the opposite pattern: respiratory error 0.45 bpm (light skin) vs 0.80 bpm (dark skin) (Srestha & Kim, Frontiers in Physiology 2026); a classical POS baseline reached MAE 2.8 / 9.5 / 16.8 / 17.8 bpm across four cohorts (Kaur, Biomedical Optics Express 2026); Fitzpatrick type VI carried OR 5.08 for measurement failure and systolic sensitivity 0.00 in a face-based BP study (Dasa & Davies, BMJ Open 2026).

**Who fails it:** Naluri (~US$20M raised, zero physiological sensing — human coaches over chat). Pulse by Prudential (rented Babylon symptom-checker, now defunct). AIA Vitality (steps arithmetic only). BookDoc / Activ@Work / KOSPEN@Activ (steps). MalaysianCalorie, Berry Best, MyKalori (food photos only). No Malaysian app measures a pulse. Globally, Binah.ai / NuraLogix-style face-scan SDKs chose the modality that fails on the exact demographic Hybrid serves.

**Hybrid play:** 'PULSE CHECK' — a 60-second morning reading on /hud: finger over the rear camera, torch forced on, green-channel mean per frame, bandpass 0.7–3.5 Hz, peak detection to inter-beat intervals. Runs fully on-device in the PWA, zero server round-trip (which also matters on Malaysian prepaid data). Gate it with a live signal-quality bar and refuse to record below threshold rather than returning a guess. Explicitly market 'works the same on every skin tone' — that is a true, evidenced, and locally resonant claim no competitor can make.

---

### [4/5 · medium] Respiratory rate is the most accurate hardware-free vital sign in the literature and essentially nobody in consumer fitness ships it — despite it being a leading indicator of illness and heat strain.

**Lens:** HARDWARE-FREE SENSING — how close is WHOOP-class readiness with zero hardware?

BOTTOM LINE: A phone gets you roughly 70% of WHOOP's readiness stack, and 0% of the 30% that constitutes WHOOP's actual moat. Chasing the remaining 30% is the trap. Inverting the axis is the win.

WHAT IS GENUINELY WHOOP-CLASS TODAY:
• Heart rate via CONTACT fingertip PPG (finger over rear lens + torch on): 1.6% ± 4.3% MAPE vs reference in 95 subjects (Google Health, Communications Medicine 2022). Critically, there was NO skin-tone gradient — 1.8% (very light–intermediate), 1.3% (tan/brown), 1.8% (dark) — because the torch transilluminates the finger rather than reflecting off melanin. For a Malaysian gym this is decisive.
• Respiratory rate from front camera, subject sitting still: MAE 0.78 ± 0.61 breaths/min (n=50), holding at 0.80 ± 0.60 in people with chronic respiratory disease. WHOOP charges money for this number.
• Resting HR trend: trivially reachable, and one of the more robust illness/overreaching signals in existence.

WHAT IS NOT:
• HRV. A camera-PPG app produced 17.49% MAPE on RMSSD against 2.16% for a Polar chest strap in 37 trained athletes (Johansson et al., Frontiers in Physiology 2025). Day-to-day biological variation in lnRMSSD is only ~3.1–5.8% (Flatt 2019). So a SINGLE camera HRV reading is mostly measurement noise — it becomes signal only as a 7-day rolling mean (√7 ≈ 2.6× noise reduction). Frequency-domain HRV (LF/HF) is unusable: it destabilises at 10% data degradation while median IBI survives to 60%.
• FACIAL rPPG in a gym. Three independent failure modes stack exactly where Hybrid operates: melanin (respiratory error 0.45 bpm light-skinned vs 0.80 dark-skinned; Fitzpatrick VI carried OR 5.08 for measurement failure in a BP study), elevated heart rate (5 of 8 rPPG methods degraded significantly at high HR — and notably low illumination mattered LESS than high HR, per the CHILL dataset, npj Digital Medicine 2025), and motion/sweat. The pretty demo (point front camera at face between rounds) is the single worst engineering choice available. Use the finger and the torch.
• Sleep staging and continuous in-session HR. Structurally unreachable — both require a device on the body while the user is not holding one. Do not fake these.

THE INVERSION — the signature that runs through the entire pose literature:
Cameras measure TIME, FREQUENCY and VELOCITY well. They measure MAGNITUDE, ABSOLUTE POSITION and DEPTH badly. Evidence is remarkably consistent: tremor peak frequency was accurate while tremor amplitude was "insufficient for clinical application"; markerless boxing showed elbow position errors >3 cm while segment velocities showed "good-to-excellent agreement"; MediaPipe knee flexion MAE 3.15–4.10° but ankle "poor concordance"; even two-camera MediaPipe 3D RMSE ranged 7.88 mm (knee medial-lateral) to 54.55 mm (ankle anterior-posterior); gait symmetry was the single weakest derived metric ("moderate to weak"). Single-camera z-depth is not usable, full stop.

That signature points at exactly one readiness marker that is combat-specific AND better on a phone than on any wrist strap: PUNCH VELOCITY DECAY. Fatigue significantly reduced punch velocity and impact force in MMA fighters (p<0.01), and 2D video agreed with 3D infrared motion capture at r = 0.964–0.999, ICC 0.81–0.99. WHOOP cannot see this. Neither can any Malaysian competitor — no Malaysian app does movement CV at all.

THE BIGGEST WIN IS NOT A SENSOR:
Lab-condition MediaPipe rep counting hits 97.2% accuracy (MAE 0.06 reps). The same task with real users placing their own phones hits 61.1% for push-ups and 61.5% for squats (JMIR mHealth 2026, n=44). Within that same study, correct diagonal framing at 1.8–2.0 m recovered 85.7–95.5% (MAE 0.05–0.28), while side-on at 90 cm gave 0% detection and front-on at 360 cm gave 20%. A 36-point accuracy gap is sitting in camera-placement UX, not in the model. This is the highest-ROI engineering work available to a solo dev, and it costs a reticle and a distance check.

VERDICT ON "WHOOP-CLASS READINESS WITH ZERO HARDWARE": Reachable for heart rate, respiratory rate and resting-HR trend. Reachable-with-caveats for HRV if and only if you ship 7-day rolling means and refuse to display daily numbers. Unreachable for sleep and continuous strain. But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** Front-camera video of a person sitting still gave respiratory rate MAE 0.78 ± 0.61 breaths/min (n=50), holding at 0.70 ± 0.67 in healthy participants and 0.80 ± 0.60 in those with chronic respiratory conditions (Communications Medicine 2022). Video-based RR methods reached RMSE 1.1 bpm / MAE 0.7 bpm / bias 0.00 bpm in a controlled animal validation (Scientific Reports 2025). This is materially better relative accuracy than camera HRV. By contrast, audio-derived RR degrades hard with effort and ambient noise: MAE 1.7 bpm overall but 3.8 bpm at 12 km/h, LOA -0.24 ± 5.07 bpm (Biosensors 2023).

**Who fails it:** WHOOP treats respiratory rate as a premium differentiator. No Malaysian app measures it. Naluri, BookDoc, MySejahtera, AIA+ — none. Samsung Health does not. It is a free, validated, high-signal metric lying unclaimed.

**Hybrid play:** Fold a 30-second respiratory-rate capture into the same morning check (front camera, chest/shoulder micro-motion, subject seated and still — the exact validated protocol). Flag elevated RR against the athlete's own baseline as an illness/heat-strain sentinel. Malaysia-specific hook: during transboundary haze season, an elevated personal RR baseline is a genuinely useful, locally meaningful alert that no fitness app anywhere provides.

---

THE BIGGEST WIN IS NOT A SENSOR:

The same task with real users placing their own phones hits 61.1% for push-ups and 61.5% for squats (JMIR mHealth 2026, n=44). Reachable-with-caveats for HRV if and only if you ship 7-day rolling means and refuse to display daily numbers. Unreachable for sleep and continuous strain. But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** JMIR mHealth 2026 (n=44, mean age 20.3, mean BMI 23.2): mean detection rate was 61.1% (SD 48.8%) for push-ups (MAE 1.08 reps) and 61.5% (SD 48.7%) for squats (MAE 1.11 reps). But within the same study, diagonal views at 90–180 cm reached 85.7% detection (MAE 0.28) for push-ups and diagonal at 200 cm reached 95.5% (MAE 0.05) for squats — while front at 360 cm gave 20% (MAE 2.70) and side at 90 cm gave 0% detection (MAE 5.0). For comparison, lab-condition MediaPipe rep counting achieves 97.2% accuracy, mean per-exercise 98.8% (range 95–100%), and viewpoint-invariant methods reach MAE 0.06 with off-by-one accuracy 0.94.

**Who fails it:** Every camera-based fitness app: Peloton Guide, Tempo, Kemtai, Zenia, Onyx, and every MediaPipe demo on the app stores. Berry Best's 'Gym Mode' is a manual set/weight logger with no camera at all. Vibefam, Mindbody and Glofox track bookings. Nobody has shipped a placement coach because it is unglamorous.

**Hybrid play:** 'RACK POSITION' — an AR framing reticle in the Shadow Dojo that will not arm the session until the phone is inside the validated envelope: diagonal (not side, not front), 1.8–2.0 m, full body in frame. Use the pose landmark bounding box to derive distance, and refuse-with-reason rather than silently degrading. Store a per-exercise placement preset so the athlete reproduces the same geometry every session (which also makes session-over-session comparison legitimate). Pair with a forced torch prompt in dim gyms and large tap targets for sweaty hands. This is the single highest-ROI item in this report: it roughly halves rep-count error for the cost of a reticle and a distance check.

Reachable-with-caveats for HRV if and only if you ship 7-day rolling means and refuse to display daily numbers. Unreachable for sleep and continuous strain. But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** MEASUREMENT: flight-time jump height error is 1.4% at 120 Hz, 0.7% at 240 Hz, 0.5% at 480 Hz, 0.3% at 1000 Hz, against test-retest variability of ~3% (Biology of Sport 2023). Browsers cannot access 120/240 fps slow-motion capture — getUserMedia on mid-range Android realistically delivers 30–60 fps, extrapolating to ~2.8–5.6% technical error, i.e. at or above the biological noise floor. Even native apps carry bias: an AI-based My Jump Lab overestimated by +2.81 cm vs force platform (p<0.001) with between-session ICC 0.89 and CV 4.2% (JSCR 2026); 95% LOA of -3.26 to 2.28 cm elsewhere (Frontiers 2026, n=76). CONSTRUCT: 'CMJ parameters remain unchanged following non-eccentrically loaded exhaustive exercise' despite significant prolonged low-frequency force depression, explicitly questioning 'the utility of CMJ as a fatigue assessment tool' (European Journal of Applied Physiology 2025). Countermovement rebound jump average power was more sensitive to acute fatigue at 24 h than CMJ height (p=0.001, d=0.67 vs p=0.02, d=-0.49) (JSCR 2026).

**Who fails it:** My Jump Lab, VertVision, Jumpster, DeepSport and every 'vertical jump AI' app treat jump height as the headline. Coaches worldwide use CMJ height as a daily readiness gate on evidence that does not support it.

**Hybrid play:** DO NOT BUILD jump height as a readiness gate — this is a 'decide not to build' finding worth as much as any feature. If a jump test is wanted, ship a rebound/reactive protocol scored on GROUND CONTACT TIME and the flight-to-contact ratio: those are TIMING metrics, which is the class cameras handle well, and they are the metrics with demonstrated fatigue sensitivity. Frame it in the HUD as 'REACTIVE INDEX', not 'jump height'. Reallocate the saved effort to Velocity Floor and Rack Position.

### [4/5 · medium] The microphone is the most under-used sensor on the phone and it works in exactly the conditions where the camera fails — pocket, dark, no framing, no propping. Zero fitness products use it for combat training.

But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** Absolute respiratory rate from audio is marginal in a gym: MAE 1.7 bpm overall but rising to 3.8 bpm at 12 km/h running, with LOA -0.24 ± 5.07 bpm and errors 'increasing with the increase in exercise intensity and ambient noise' (Biosensors 2023, n=10); a smartphone-microphone method reached RMSE 2.2 bpm / MAE 1.5 bpm and was the only method failing an R² > 0.97 threshold (Scientific Reports 2025). BUT impact transients are a fundamentally different and far easier signal-processing problem than breath sounds: heavy-bag strikes, barbell rack contacts and foot strikes are high-amplitude, broadband, short-duration events with high SNR even in a noisy room, detectable by simple onset detection rather than learned models. Note also the rep-counting literature confirms high accuracy is achievable from non-visual streams: IMU-based counting achieved within-1-repetition accuracy in 91% of sets and 99.96% exercise classification.

**Who fails it:** Nothing in the market. Every camera fitness app requires the phone propped and framed, which is a real friction point on a crowded gym floor and impossible for bag work where the athlete is moving around the bag. No product treats audio as a first-class training sensor.

**Hybrid play:** 'ROUND EARS' — a microphone-only heavy-bag mode. Phone in pocket or on a bench, screen off. Detect strike onsets, output strike count, strikes per minute, and — the valuable part — strike-RATE DECAY across the round, which is a work-output fatigue curve requiring no camera, no framing, negligible battery and zero data. Also use silence-detection to auto-time rest intervals, killing manual timer taps with sweaty hands. Do NOT attempt absolute breathing rate from audio in a gym; the evidence does not support it. This is the perfect complement to camera modes: it works precisely where they don't.

---

Frequency-domain HRV (LF/HF) is unusable: it destabilises at 10% data degradation while median IBI survives to 60%.
• FACIAL rPPG in a gym. Three independent failure modes stack exactly where Hybrid operates: melanin (respiratory error 0.45 bpm light-skinned vs 0.80 dark-skinned; Fitzpatrick VI carried OR 5.08 for measurement failure in a BP study), elevated heart rate (5 of 8 rPPG methods degraded significantly at high HR — and notably low illumination mattered LESS than high HR, per the CHILL dataset, npj Digital Medicine 2025), and motion/sweat. The pretty demo (point front camera at face between rounds) is the single worst engineering choice available. Use the finger and the torch.
• Sleep staging and continuous in-session HR. Structurally unreachable — both require a device on the body while the user is not holding one. Do not fake these.

THE INVERSION — the signature that runs through the entire pose literature:
Cameras measure TIME, FREQUENCY and VELOCITY well. They measure MAGNITUDE, ABSOLUTE POSITION and DEPTH badly. Evidence is remarkably consistent: tremor peak frequency was accurate while tremor amplitude was "insufficient for clinical application"; markerless boxing showed elbow position errors >3 cm while segment velocities showed "good-to-excellent agreement"; MediaPipe knee flexion MAE 3.15–4.10° but ankle "poor concordance"; even two-camera MediaPipe 3D RMSE ranged 7.88 mm (knee medial-lateral) to 54.55 mm (ankle anterior-posterior); gait symmetry was the single weakest derived metric ("moderate to weak"). Single-camera z-depth is not usable, full stop.

That signature points at exactly one readiness marker that is combat-specific AND better on a phone than on any wrist strap: PUNCH VELOCITY DECAY. Fatigue significantly reduced punch velocity and impact force in MMA fighters (p<0.01), and 2D video agreed with 3D infrared motion capture at r = 0.964–0.999, ICC 0.81–0.99. WHOOP cannot see this. Neither can any Malaysian competitor — no Malaysian app does movement CV at all.

THE BIGGEST WIN IS NOT A SENSOR:
Lab-condition MediaPipe rep counting hits 97.2% accuracy (MAE 0.06 reps). The same task with real users placing their own phones hits 61.1% for push-ups and 61.5% for squats (JMIR mHealth 2026, n=44). Within that same study, correct diagonal framing at 1.8–2.0 m recovered 85.7–95.5% (MAE 0.05–0.28), while side-on at 90 cm gave 0% detection and front-on at 360 cm gave 20%. A 36-point accuracy gap is sitting in camera-placement UX, not in the model. This is the highest-ROI engineering work available to a solo dev, and it costs a reticle and a distance check.

VERDICT ON "WHOOP-CLASS READINESS WITH ZERO HARDWARE": Reachable for heart rate, respiratory rate and resting-HR trend. Reachable-with-caveats for HRV if and only if you ship 7-day rolling means and refuse to display daily numbers. Unreachable for sleep and continuous strain. But the honest framing is that WHOOP-class readiness is the wrong prize — performance-derived readiness (what you can DO today vs your baseline: punch velocity, bar velocity, tempo decay) is measurable, validated, combat-specific, and structurally unavailable to every strap-based competitor.

**Evidence:** Self-reported stress and nervousness showed NO association with HRV (p = 0.63 and p = 0.41) across 39 adults tracked for 3 months, and feeling energized had a NEGATIVE association with HRV (p < 0.01); authors cautioned that 'subjective feelings of readiness may not correspond to activity tracker biometrics' (Sensors 2026). Yet both predict performance independently: lnRMSSD (β = -0.019, p = 0.003) AND perceived recovery status (β = -0.014, p = 0.008) each predicted sprint time in 56 sprint athletes (Sensors 2026). Correlations between subjective readiness and wearable data ranged r = 0.39–0.81 with high individual variability, and 'personalized analyses proved more accurate than group averages' (Sports Medicine – Open 2025). Session-RPE is specifically validated in boxing — measurable as early as 10 minutes post-session across easy/moderate/hard intensities — and predicted internal load in 21 female collegiate boxers at r = 0.426–0.880, though it failed against Stagno's TRIMP (r = 0.181) and against Edwards TL in karate technical training (r = 0.53, p = 0.18), so it is context-dependent, not universal.

**Who fails it:** WHOOP and Oura present a single fused 'recovery score', hiding disagreement between inputs and destroying the most informative signal. Malaysian apps collect no subjective data at all — Naluri's is a talk-therapy product, AIA Vitality is points arithmetic. Vibefam and Mindbody log attendance.

**Hybrid play:** 'SPLIT READOUT' — a 3-tap pre-session input (sleep, soreness, motivation) weighted EQUALLY with camera physiology, and when the two disagree, SAY SO on the HUD rather than averaging it away: 'BODY SAYS GREEN / YOU SAY RED — TRUST YOURSELF, CUT VOLUME 20%.' This is nearly free to build, is better evidenced than the fused-score approach, and is a genuinely novel UI in a category where everyone hides their uncertainty. It also degrades gracefully: on a day the athlete skips the camera check, the product still has a valid readiness input.

---

### [3/5 · medium] The entire international gym-SaaS stack is structurally locked out of Malaysia on payment rails, leaving the member-performance layer unclaimed by default — and the one local incumbent has explicitly not built it

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** Fetched PushPress pricing: Free / $159 / $229 per month plus Branded App $81–97/mo and Train (workout tracking) $79/mo, with processing quoted only as 'CC' and 'ACH' — US rails. Glofox's pricing page is quote-only ('Get a price quote'), i.e. sales-led enterprise motion. Hapana publishes no monthly price at all and gates it behind a demo. Meanwhile Vibefam's own Malaysian martial-arts page pitches against Mindbody ('enterprise-scale pricing, a complex interface, slower support') and Glofox ('annual contracts and limited local payment-rail support'), and lists its martial-arts feature set as 'belt-rank class scheduling' plus on-floor check-in — with no member performance tracking, no white-label member app and no retention analytics mentioned anywhere on the page. A full PushPress-equivalent stack (Pro + Branded App + Train) is ~$319/mo, roughly RM1,400/mo at ~4.4 FX, and still delivers zero pose, zero OCR, zero Malaysian food.

**Who fails it:** PushPress, Glofox, Mindbody, Zen Planner, Hapana — all card/ACH-billed and USD/EUR-priced, unusable as the billing spine for a Damansara gym. Vibefam wins locally on FPX/GrabPay/Alipay/WeChat Pay but ships an owner-facing ops tool: the member app is a booking shell, and rank is metadata a coach declares, never anything the athlete demonstrably did.

**Hybrid play:** Position HUD explicitly as a *performance layer*, never a management platform. Ship a one-way import (CSV, then API) of roster + class timetable + membership status from Vibefam/Mindbody/Google Sheets, and never touch billing or booking. Sell at RM250–450/gym/month — an order of magnitude under the RM1,400/mo foreign stack and additive to the RM-priced local one. Pitch Vibefam as a partner, not a rival: they have 700+ studios and a hole exactly the shape of this product.

---

### [3/5 · small] A gym's largest unmet operating cost is marketing content, and HUD sits on the only proprietary content supply in the building — but nothing in the market turns member data into publishable assets on a schedule

**Lens:** BUSINESS MODEL AND MOAT — "one gym" is not a small market, it is a *complete* one. Every global fitness app is structurally forced to be right about strangers it will never meet, on equipment it has never seen, eating food it cannot identify, with no human backstop. Hybrid Combative HUD is wrong about none of those things: it knows the six bags, the eleven machines, the Tuesday 8pm Muay Thai roster, the coach's name, and the fifteen mamak stalls within walking distance of the door. The moat is not the code — a funded team could clone the pose engine in a quarter. The moat is (a) a coach standing on the floor who converts every uncertain AI output into verified ground truth, (b) a bounded physical world that makes accuracy an engineering problem instead of a research problem, and (c) the fact that CAC is zero because 100% of the addressable market walks through one door. Monetisation must follow the Malaysian evidence, not Silicon Valley instinct: the consumer wallet is dead (AIA Vitality anchors "wellness layer" at RM10/mo and pays you back; KFit's RM99 unlimited model failed outright), so the member never sees a charge. The gym pays, priced against churn saved in MYR — the only KPI a gym owner actually has. And critically: do NOT rebuild booking and billing. The US SaaS stack (PushPress, Glofox, Zen Planner, Hapana) cannot bill a Malaysian gym at all — their rails are card/ACH — which leaves Vibefam effectively unopposed locally, and Vibefam's entire martial-arts differentiation is "belt-rank class scheduling" with no member performance tracking whatsoever. That is the whitespace: be the performance layer that sits ON TOP of whatever the gym already runs, in every gym, not the booking layer in one.

**Evidence:** KL boutique studios market on Instagram and TikTok, and the research notes Activ@Work's smartest mechanic is bonus points for submitting a monthly group activity photo — social proof, manually harvested. TRIBE, Monarchy, Panthera all run content-led acquisition. PushPress's nearest equivalent is 'Member Intel' surfacing 'milestones... worth acting on' — it identifies the moment but produces nothing publishable. /brag already generates Instagram story cards for members but generates nothing for the *gym*.

**Who fails it:** PushPress (surfaces moments, no asset), Hapana/Glofox/Mindbody (campaign tooling with no content), Vibefam (ops only), and every consumer app, whose sharing output is branded for the app rather than the venue.

**Hybrid play:** Point the existing canvas pipeline at the owner. Every Monday, auto-generate a gym content pack: the week's leaderboard card, three member milestone cards pre-approved for posting, a coach shout-out card, and the Combat Ladder standing — all gym-branded, all ready to post or paste into the members' WhatsApp group. Add a member consent toggle so nothing publishes without permission. This converts a data product into a marketing department, which is a line item a gym owner already pays for in time or cash, and it makes the weekly ladder a recurring public artefact that markets the gym to rivals — feeding the cross-gym distribution loop.

---

### [3/5 · small] When a full training history sits in an LLM's context, the interface stops being dashboards you must interpret and becomes questions you can ask. Every app here hands you charts; none can answer 'why was my left hook slower in July?'

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. Six of the Malaysian nutrition apps and four of the regional ones do food-photo-to-macros — and the NIH/ASN 2026 study measured all four leading photo apps underestimating meal energy by roughly 33% (Cal AI worst at -345 kcal). HealthifyMe's own stated Snap accuracy is 60-75% on its home cuisine. Meanwhile the capabilities that are genuinely superhuman are entirely unclaimed in Malaysia and thinly claimed globally.

A human coach's binding constraints are: (a) they are present for ~3 hours of your week and zero of your meals; (b) their sensing resolution is one pair of eyes at ~10Hz, watching one athlete at a time; (c) their memory is recency-weighted anecdote, not n=400 sessions; (d) they cannot run the future before you live it; (e) their income depends on you, which caps their honesty. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.

Four structural advantages, in order of defensibility for a solo dev with a phone:

1. RESOLUTION. The phone already contains a sensor 1,400x faster than its camera — the microphone — and no fitness product in this corpus uses it. Combat sport is an impact sport; impacts are acoustic transients. 30fps vision literally skips the contact frame of a jab.
2. PRESENCE. Being at every rep AND every meal for the same person for years makes N-of-1 causal inference possible, which is the one thing no dietitian or coach can do. Critically, this does NOT require accurate calories — a systematic bias cancels in a within-subject model. The category is losing an accuracy war it did not need to fight.
3. RECALL + CROSS-REFERENCE. Longitudinal per-joint baselining turns pain from a lagging indicator into a leading one, and turns a gym of 100 bodies into a longitudinal cohort study with known outcomes.
4. SIMULATION. Monte-Carlo-ing an 8-week block against the athlete's own adherence hazard model and Malaysia's deterministic calendar (Ramadan, Raya kampung week, CNY, Deepavali, monsoon, haze) is a capability that has no human analogue at all.

A fifth advantage matters only in Malaysia and may be the commercial unlock: the research shows every behavioural incentive in the country (AIA Vitality, PERKESO Activ@Work, KOSPEN@Activ, WeWard, Great Eastern) is step-based, and AIA specifically pays only for workout data auto-captured by a linked app or device — manual entries earn nothing. A camera+mic that objectively counts 412 strikes over 47 minutes IS auto-captured sensor data. Hybrid can be the wearable. That is the only route to being net cash-positive for the user, which the RM10/mo AIA anchor and KFit's RM99 failure make mandatory.

Architecture consequence: everything continuous and identity-bearing — pose, audio, PPG — stays on-device, both because mid-range Android on prepaid data cannot afford otherwise and because PDPA-era Malaysians will not send body video offshore. The cloud sees only compact structured digests. That split is simultaneously the privacy story, the cost story, and the reason a solo dev can actually ship this.

Inverted priority: the research is right that /fuel photo-to-macros is the commodity and Shadow Dojo is the differentiated asset — but the sharpest version of that insight is that /fuel's value is not its accuracy, it is that it is the second half of a causal pair nobody else in this market possesses on the same user ID.

**Evidence:** Berry Best ships a chat coach with no training telemetry behind it. HealthifyMe's Ria answers generic nutrition questions. MyKalori's insight — that the winning interface in Malaysia may be conversational and require no app at all — is validated by the corpus's strongest behavioural finding: WhatsApp is the operating system of Malaysian gym life, and 'a product that produces something worth pasting INTO a WhatsApp group wins.' Real usage is code-switched Manglish. No product in this market has a chat surface with a genuine multimodal personal history behind it, because none of them has the history.

**Who fails it:** Berry Best, HealthifyMe/Ria, Naluri (human coaches over chat, no telemetry), every gym app. Strava's Athlete Intelligence is the closest analogue globally and only summarises GPS metrics.

**Hybrid play:** An '/ask' surface where the model receives a compact structured digest of the member's whole history — session summaries, kinematic invariants, pain events, meal features, calendar — rather than raw rows. Three design rules from the corpus: accept code-switched Manglish input without translating the UI; make every answer a pasteable block of text sized for a WhatsApp group, not an in-app chart; and degrade honestly offline by serving the cached digest and saying so rather than hallucinating. Cost note that matters for a solo dev: a monthly digest plus on-demand queries is a few thousand tokens, versus a vision call per meal — this is the cheap AI, not the expensive one.

---

### [3/5 · small] A coach learns you skipped after you skipped. A model can act at 4pm on the day you are statistically about to skip, at the single moment of maximum leverage, offering a smaller session rather than guilt.

**Lens:** AI CAPABILITY FRONTIER — what a machine can do that a human coach structurally cannot, and which nobody in this market is doing.

The central finding: every dollar of "AI" in this market is pointed at the one capability that is both commoditised and provably broken. Those five constraints define the frontier. Everything below attacks one of them.
